3.2

Proof of Proposition
3.2
.

By adding and subtracting terms, we have

\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}^{2}-\sigma_{0}^{2}=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{2}+P_{0}(\phi_{n}^{2}-\phi_{0}^{2}).

The second term is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. For the first term, for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{2}}\right|\geq\epsilon,\,\phi_{n}^{2}\in\mathscr{G}\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\,\phi_{n}^{2}\notin\mathscr{G}\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathscr{G}}\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})g}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(\phi_{n}^{2}\notin\mathscr{G}\right).

Since
\mathscr{G}
is a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class, the first term is
o(1)
, and since
\phi_{n}^{2}\in\mathscr{G}
with probability tending to one the second term is
o(1)
. Hence,
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{2}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, which completes the proof of the first statement.

By Theorem 2.10.14 of
van der Vaart & Wellner, (1996)
, since
\sup_{f\in\mathscr{F}}|P_{0}f|<\infty
, condition
(A1)
implies that
\mathscr{F}^{2}=\{f^{2}:f\in\mathscr{F}\}
is
P_{0}
-Glivenko-Cantelli in probability, and since
P_{0}^{*}(\phi_{n}^{2}\in\mathscr{F}^{2})\to 1
, this implies (i). By Cauchy-Schwarz and Minkowski’s inequalities, we also have

\displaystyle\left|P_{0}(\phi_{n}^{2}-\phi_{0}^{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}|\phi_{n}^{2}-\phi_{0}^{2}|

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left|(\phi_{n}-\phi_{0})(\phi_{n}+\phi_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\phi_{n}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\left\|{\phi_{n}+\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\phi_{n}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}[\left\|{\phi_{n}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+2\left\|{\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}],

which is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by
(A2)
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Supplement B
Proof of results in Section
3

See
3.5

Proof of Lemma
3.5
.

A standard way to prove results of this type outside the setting of the bootstrap is the continuous mapping theorem (see, e.g.,
van der Vaart,, 2000
, Theorem 19.24
). However, the continuous mapping theorem might not be applicable in the bootstrap setting as the map
(w_{1},\dotsc,w_{n})\mapsto h(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n},w_{1},\dotsc,w_{n}))
might not be measurable given almost
x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n}
for all
h\in C_{b}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F}))
.

For any
\tau>0
, we define
\mathscr{F}_{\tau}=\{f-f_{\infty}:f\in\mathscr{F},\rho(f,f_{\infty})<\tau\}
. We then have

\displaystyle\left\{|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(f_{n}^{*}-f_{\infty})|\geq\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(f_{n}^{*}-f_{\infty})|\geq\varepsilon,\ \rho(f_{n}^{*},f_{\infty})<\tau,\ f_{n}^{*}\in\mathscr{F}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cup\left\{\rho(f_{n}^{*},f_{\infty})\geq\tau\ \text{or}\ f_{n}^{*}\notin\mathscr{F}\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\rho(f_{n}^{*},f_{\infty})\geq\tau\right\}\cup\left\{f_{n}^{*}\notin\mathscr{F}\right\}

for any
\varepsilon,\tau>0
. Thus,

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(f_{n}^{*}-f_{\infty})|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq P^{*}_{W}\left(\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right)+P^{*}_{W}\left(\rho(f_{n}^{*},f_{\infty})\geq\tau\right)+P^{*}_{W}\left(f_{n}^{*}\notin\mathscr{F}\right).

The second and third terms on the right-hand side conditionally converge to zero in outer probability for any
\tau>0
by assumption.
Hence, the proof is complete if we can show that for all
\varepsilon,\delta,\gamma>0
there exists
\tau>0
such that
P_{0}^{*}\left(P^{*}_{W}\left(\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right)>\delta\right)<\gamma
for all
n
large enough.

For each
\varepsilon,\tau>0
there exists a sequence of functions
h_{m}:\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{R}
such that
h_{m}
is
m
-Lipschitz for all
m
,
1\{\|z\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\}\leq h_{m}(z)\leq 1
for all
z\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F})
and
m
, and
h_{m}(z)
monotonically decreases to
1\{\|z\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\}
as
m\to\infty
for each
z\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F})
. For instance,
h_{m}:z\mapsto\min\{\max(m[\|z\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}-\varepsilon]+1,0),1\}
satisfies these criteria. For any
\varepsilon,\tau>0
and
m\in\{1,2,\dots\}
, we can now write

\displaystyle P^{*}_{W}\left(\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\left[E^{*}_{W}1\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right\}-E^{*}_{W}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*})\right]+\left[E^{*}_{W}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*})-E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\left[E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})-E_{0}1\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right\}\right]+P_{0}\left(\left\|{\mathbb{G}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left[E^{*}_{W}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*})-E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})\right]+\left[E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})-E_{0}1\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+P_{0}\left(\left\|{\mathbb{G}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right),

where the second inequality follows because
E^{*}_{W}1\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right\}\leq E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\right)
for all
\varepsilon,\tau>0
and
m\in\{1,2,\dots\}
by assumption. For the final term on the right-hand side, since almost all sample paths of
\mathbb{G}_{0}
are uniformly continuous in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F})
with respect to
\rho
,
for any
\varepsilon,\delta>0
, we can choose
\tau>0
such that
P_{0}\left(\left\|{\mathbb{G}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right)<\delta
. For the second term on the right-hand side, by the monotone convergence theorem,
\lim_{m\to\infty}E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})=E_{0}1\{\|\mathbb{G}_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F_{\tau}}}\geq\varepsilon\}
. Hence, for any
\varepsilon,\delta,\tau>0
, we can choose
m
such that
E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})-E_{0}1\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\tau}}\geq\varepsilon\right\}<\delta
. Finally, for the first term on the right-hand side, since
h_{m}
is bounded and
m
-Lipschitz,
h_{m}/m\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F}))
for each
m
. Therefore, by the assumed conditional weak convergence of
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}
to
\mathbb{G}_{0}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F})
,
E^{*}_{W}\frac{1}{m}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\right)\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}E_{0}\frac{1}{m}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})
for each
m
, so that
E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\right)\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})
as well. Hence, for any
\varepsilon,\delta,\gamma,\tau>0
and
m\in\{1,2,\dots\}
,
P_{0}^{*}\left(E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h_{m}(\mathbb{G}_{0})>\delta\right)<\gamma
for all
n
large enough. This completes the proof.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.4

Proof of Theorem
3.4
.

We let
h
be an arbitrary element of
\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
. By adding and subtracting terms and the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(n^{1/2}\left[T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\right]\right)-E_{0}h\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)}\right|\\
&\qquad\leq E_{W}^{*}\left|{h\left(n^{1/2}\left[T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\right]\right)-h\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\right)}\right|+\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\right)-E_{0}h\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)}\right|.\end{split}

(15)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
15
), we note that for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\left|{h(x_{1})-h(x_{2})}\right|\leq 2\wedge|x_{1}-x_{2}|
, and for any
\varepsilon>0
,
2\wedge|x_{1}-x_{2}|\leq\varepsilon+2I(|x_{1}-x_{2}|>\varepsilon)
, which implies that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{W}^{*}\left|{h\left(n^{1/2}\left[T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\right]\right)-h\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\right)}\right|\\
&\qquad\leq\varepsilon+2P^{*}_{W}\left(\left|n^{1/2}\left[T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\right]-\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right).\end{split}

(16)

We now write

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\left[T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\right]-\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0})+n^{1/2}\left[T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0})+n^{1/2}R_{n}^{*}.

Note that
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by conditions
(B1)
–
(B2)
and
Lemma

3.5
, and
n^{1/2}R_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by condition
(B3)
. This implies the first term on the right-hand side of (
15
) is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
.
For the second term on the right hand side of (
15
), we define the function
g:\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbb{R}
as
g(z):=h(z(\phi_{0}))
. For any
z_{1},z_{2}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F})
with

\|z_{1}-z_{2}\|_{\mathscr{F}}>0
, we have

\frac{|g(z_{1})-g(z_{2})|}{\|z_{1}-z_{2}\|_{\mathscr{F}}}=\frac{|h(z_{1}(\phi_{0}))-h(z_{2}(\phi_{0}))|}{\|z_{1}-z_{2}\|_{\mathscr{F}}}\leq\frac{|z_{1}(\phi_{0})-z_{2}(\phi_{0})|}{\|z_{1}-z_{2}\|_{\mathscr{F}}}\leq 1

because
h\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
\phi_{0}\in\mathscr{F}
. Hence,
g\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F}))
. Therefore,

\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\right)-E_{0}h\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)}\right|\leq\sup_{g\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F}))}\left|{E_{W}^{*}g\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}g\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\right)}\right|\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0

by condition
(B1)
. This implies conditional asymptotic linearity of
T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})
. Conditional weak convergence of
n^{1/2}\left[T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\right]
is then implied by (
16
).

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.7

Proof of
Lemma

3.7
.

We argue along subsequences using a standard argument structure. By Lemma 1.9.2 of
van der Vaart & Wellner, (1996)
, every subsequence
\{n_{k}:k=1,2,\dots\}
has a further subsequence
\{n_{k_{l}}:l=1,2,\dots\}
such

\sup_{f,g\in\mathscr{F}}\left|\|f-g\|_{L_{2}(\hat{P}_{n_{k_{l}}})}-\|f-g\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{a.s.*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

By Theorem 4.5 of
Sheehy & Wellner, (1992)
, we then have
n_{k_{l}}^{1/2}(\mathbb{P}_{n_{k_{l}}}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n_{k_{l}}})\underset{W}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{a.s.*}}}{{\leadsto}}}\mathbb{G}_{0}
. Hence, every
\{n_{k}\}
has a further subsequence
\{n_{k_{l}}\}
such that

\sup_{h\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F}))}\left|{E_{W}^{*}h(\mathbb{G}_{n_{k_{l}}}^{*})-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0})}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{a.s.*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

which by Lemma 1.9.2 of
van der Vaart & Wellner, (1996)
again implies that

\sup_{h\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F}))}\left|{E_{W}^{*}h(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*})-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0})}\right|\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0,

i.e.,
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}
.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.8

Proof of Proposition
3.8
.

Since
\mathscr{F}
has uniformly bounded envelope function and finite uniform entropy integral, by Theorem 2.8.3 of
van der Vaart & Wellner, (1996)
, conditions (i)–(ii) of Lemma
3.7
are satisfied. We next show the uniform convergence of semi-metric. For simplicity, we denote
\mathscr{F}_{\infty}:=\{f-g:f,g\in\mathscr{F}\}
and
\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2}:=\{(f-g)^{2}:f,g\in\mathscr{F}\}
. Since
|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\leq\sqrt{|x-y|}
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{f,g\in\mathscr{F}}\left|{\left\|{f-g}\right\|_{L_{2}(\hat{P}_{n})}-\left\|{f-g}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}}\right|\leq\sup_{f,g\in\mathscr{F}}\left|{\int(f-g)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})}\right|^{1/2}

Hence, condition (iii) of Lemma
3.7
is satisfied if
\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
.

We show that
P_{0}^{*}(\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2}}\geq\delta)\to 0
for all
\delta>0
using Theorem 2.2 of
Gaenssler & Rost, (2000)
. To do so, we verify their conditions (2.2)–(2.5) with
\mu_{n}=L_{n}
and
\nu_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{n}
. Since
\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2}
is uniformly bounded by the uniform boundedness of
\mathscr{F}
, conditions (2.2) and (2.3) are satisfied, as discussed following Theorem 2.2 of
Gaenssler & Rost, (2000)
.
To show condition (2.4), we firstly note that following inequality holds for all
\varepsilon>0
and measures
Q
on
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B})
,

N(4\varepsilon,\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2},L_{1}(Q))\leq N(4\varepsilon,\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2},L_{2}(Q))\leq N(2\varepsilon,\mathscr{F}_{\infty},L_{2}(Q))\leq N(\varepsilon,\mathscr{F},L_{2}(Q))^{2}.

The first inequality is because
\left\|{\,\cdot\,}\right\|_{L_{1}(Q)}\leq\left\|{\,\cdot\,}\right\|_{L_{2}(Q)}
by Hölder’s inequality. The second inequality is because for any
f_{\infty},g_{\infty}\in\mathscr{F}_{\infty}
, we have
Q(f_{\infty}^{2}-g_{\infty}^{2})^{2}=Q(f_{\infty}-g_{\infty})^{2}(f_{\infty}+g_{\infty})^{2}\leq 4Q(f_{\infty}-g_{\infty})^{2}
by uniform boundedness of
\mathscr{F}
. The third inequality is because for any
f_{1},g_{1},f_{2},g_{2}\in\mathscr{F}
, we have
Q((f_{1}-g_{1})-(f_{2}-g_{2}))^{2}\leq Q(f_{1}-f_{2})^{2}+Q(g_{1}-g_{2})^{2}+2\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{L_{2}(Q)}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{L_{2}(Q)}
. Since
\mathscr{F}
has uniformly integrable
L_{2}
entropy, we have

\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{Q}\left\{\log N(\varepsilon,\mathscr{F},L_{2}(Q))\right\}^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}\varepsilon<\infty,

where the supremum is taken over all finitely discrete probability measures
Q
on
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B})
. By exercise 1 in Chapter 2.5 of
van der Vaart & Wellner, (1996)
, the supremum over finitely discrete
Q
can be replaced by the supremum over all probability measures
Q
such that
0<QF^{2}<\infty
without changing the assumption of uniformly integrable
L_{2}
entropy of
\mathscr{F}
. So, we have

\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{Q}\left\{\log N(\varepsilon,\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2},L_{1}(Q))\right\}^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}\varepsilon<\infty,

(17)

where the supremum is taken over all probability measures
Q
on
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B})
. Since
N(\varepsilon,\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2},L_{1}(\hat{P}_{n}))\leq\sup_{Q}N(\varepsilon,\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2},L_{1}(Q))
almost surely, and the latter is finite for
\varepsilon>0
by (
17
),
N(\varepsilon,\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2},L_{1}(\hat{P}_{n}))
is stochastically bounded for all
\varepsilon>0
. Furthermore, since
\|\cdot\|_{d_{\tilde{\nu}_{n}}^{(1)}}\leq\|\cdot\|_{L_{1}(\hat{P}_{n})}
for
d_{\tilde{\nu}_{n}}^{(1)}
defined in
Gaenssler & Rost, (2000)
, this further implies that condition (2.4) of Theorem 2.2 of
Gaenssler & Rost, (2000)
holds.

Lastly, to show condition (2.5) in Theorem 2.2 of
Gaenssler & Rost, (2000)
holds, we show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{f_{\infty}\in\mathscr{F}_{\infty}}\left|{P_{0}*L_{n}(f_{\infty}^{2})-P_{0}(f_{\infty}^{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{f_{\infty}\in\mathscr{F}_{\infty}}\left|{\iint f_{\infty}(x+y)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}L_{n}(y)-\iint f_{\infty}(x+y)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\delta_{0}(y)}\right|=0.

The function
y\mapsto\int f_{\infty}^{2}(x+y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)
is uniformly bounded by 1 since
\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2}
is uniformly bounded by 1. Furthermore, for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathscr{X}
and
f_{\infty}\in\mathscr{F}_{\infty}
, using the assumption that
p_{0}
is Lipschitz, we have

\displaystyle\left|{\int f_{\infty}(x+y_{1})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)-\int f_{\infty}(x+y_{2})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|{\int f_{\infty}(x+y_{1})^{2}p_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\lambda(x)-\int f_{\infty}(x+y_{2})^{2}p_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\lambda(x)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|{\int f_{\infty}(z)^{2}[p_{0}(z-y_{1})-p_{0}(z-y_{2})]\,\mathrm{d}\lambda(z)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\gamma\left|{y_{1}-y_{2}}\right|\int f_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\gamma\left|{y_{1}-y_{2}}\right|\int F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda.

for some
\gamma>0
. Since
\int F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda<\infty
by assumption, this implies
y\mapsto\int f_{\infty}^{2}(x+y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)
is uniformly bounded by 1 and
\gamma^{\prime}
-Lipschitz for
\gamma^{\prime}:=4\gamma\int F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda
for all
f_{\infty}\in\mathscr{F}_{\infty}
. Thus, the function
\min(\gamma^{\prime-1},1)\times\int f_{\infty}^{2}(x+\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
, which implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{\infty}\in\mathscr{F}_{\infty}}\left|{\iint f_{\infty}(x+y)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}L_{n}(y)-\iint f_{\infty}(x+y)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\delta_{0}(y)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\min(\gamma^{\prime-1},1)}\sup_{h\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathscr{X})}\left|{\int h(y)\,\mathrm{d}L_{n}(y)-\int h(y)\,\mathrm{d}\delta_{0}(y)}\right|,

which goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
because
L_{n}
converges weakly to
\delta_{0}
by assumption. We have now checked all the conditions of Theorem 2.2 of
Gaenssler & Rost,, 2000
, so we conclude that
E_{0}^{*}\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}_{\infty}^{2}}^{p}\to 0
for all
p\geq 1
. This demonstrates that condition (iii) of Lemma
3.7
holds, so the result follows.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.11

Proof of Lemma
3.11
.

By adding and subtracting terms,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*2}-\sigma_{0}^{2}=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*2}+(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{*2}+P_{0}(\phi_{n}^{*2}-\phi_{0}^{2}).

(18)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
18
), for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}\left(\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\,\phi_{n}^{*2}\in\mathscr{G}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+P_{W}^{*}\left(\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\,\phi_{n}^{*2}\notin\mathscr{G}\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathscr{G}}\left|{(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})g}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(\phi_{n}^{*2}\notin\mathscr{G}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1).

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
18
), for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(\left|{(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{*2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}\left(\left|{(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{*2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\,\phi_{n}^{*2}\in\mathscr{G}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+P_{W}^{*}\left(\left|{(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}^{*2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\,\phi_{n}^{*2}\notin\mathscr{G}\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathscr{G}}\left|{(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})g}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(\phi_{n}^{*2}\notin\mathscr{G}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1).

Therefore,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}-\sigma_{0}^{2}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. Furthermore,
P_{0}(\phi_{n}^{*2}-\phi_{0}^{2})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
is implied by condition
(B2)
. This is because by Cauchy-Schwarz and Minkowski’s inequalities,

\displaystyle\left|{P_{0}(\phi_{n}^{*2}-\phi_{0}^{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}|\phi_{n}^{*2}-\phi_{0}^{2}|

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left|{(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}+\phi_{0})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\left\|{\phi_{n}^{*}+\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}[\left\|{\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+2\left\|{\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}]

\displaystyle=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1).

Specifically, for the empirical bootstrap
\hat{P}_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{n}
, condition
(B1)
(b) implies
\mathscr{F}
is
P_{0}
-Donsker by Theorem 3.6.1 of
van der Vaart & Wellner, (1996)
. Hence,
\mathscr{G}=\{f^{2}:f\in\mathscr{F}\}
is
P_{0}
-Glivenko-Cantelli in probability by Lemma 2.10.14 of
van der Vaart, (1991)
, which implies (ii) and (iii) by Theorem 2.6 of
Giné & Zinn, (1990)
. Meanwhile, (i) is implied by condition
(B1)
(a), where
\mathscr{G}=\{f^{2}:f\in\mathscr{F}\}
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Lemma B.1

(Bootstrap Slutsky’s Theorem)
.

Let
X_{n}^{*}
and
Y_{n}^{*}
be two sequences of real-valued random variables defined on the product probability space
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B},P_{0})^{n}\times(\mathscr{W}_{n},\mathscr{C}_{n},Q_{n})^{n}
. Assume that
X_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}X
and
Y_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}c
in
\mathbb{R}
, where
X
is a tight, Borel measurable limit and
c
is a constant. We then have (i)
Y_{n}^{*}X_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}cX
in
\mathbb{R}
, (ii)
(Y_{n}^{*})^{-1}X_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}c^{-1}X
in
\mathbb{R}
provided
c\neq 0
, and (iii)
X_{n}^{*}+Y_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}X+Y
in
\mathbb{R}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

We only show (i); a similar argument can be applied to yield (ii) and (iii). As in the proof of Lemma
3.5
, a standard way to prove results of this type outside the setting of the
bootstrap is the continuous mapping theorem (e.g., Lemma 2.8 of
van der Vaart,, 2000
). However, the continuous mapping theorem might not be applicable in the bootstrap setting as the map
(w_{1},\dotsc,w_{n})\mapsto h(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n},w_{1},\dotsc,w_{n}))
might not be measurable given almost every
x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n}
for all
h\in C_{b}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathscr{F}))
.

We let
h
be an arbitrary element of
\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
. By adding and subtracting terms and the triangle inequality, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle|E_{W}^{*}h(X_{n}^{*}Y_{n}^{*})-E_{0}h(Xc)|

\displaystyle\leq|E_{W}^{*}h(X_{n}^{*}Y_{n}^{*})-E_{W}^{*}h(X_{n}^{*}c)|+|E_{W}^{*}h(X_{n}^{*}c)-E_{0}h(Xc)|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+2P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}Y_{n}^{*}-X_{n}^{*}c|\geq\varepsilon)+|E_{W}^{*}h(X_{n}^{*}c)-E_{0}h(Xc)|,

(19)

where the second inequality is because
\left|{h(x_{1})-h(x_{2})}\right|\leq 2\wedge|x_{1}-x_{2}|\leq\varepsilon+2I(|x_{1}-x_{2}|>\varepsilon)
for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. By defining
g:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
as
g(x)=cx
, we note that
\min(|c|{-1},1)\times(h\circ g)\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
because for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
x_{1}\neq x_{2}
,

\displaystyle\frac{|\min(|c|^{-1},1)\times(h\circ g)(x_{1})-\min(|c|^{-1},1)\times(h\circ g)(x_{2})|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}

\displaystyle\qquad=\min(|c|^{-1},1)\frac{|(h\circ g)(x_{1})-(h\circ g)(x_{2})|}{|g(x_{1})-g(x_{2})|}\frac{|g(x_{1})-g(x_{2})|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\min(|c|,1)\frac{|(h\circ g)(x_{1})-(h\circ g)(x_{2})|}{|g(x_{1})-g(x_{2})|}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 1

because
h\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
. Since in addition
|\min(|c|^{-1},1)\times(h\circ g)|\leq 1
,
\min(|c|^{-1},1)\times(h\circ g)\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
. Hence, for the third term on the right-hand side of (
19
), we have

\displaystyle|E_{W}^{*}h(X_{n}^{*}c)-E_{0}h(Xc)|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\min(|c|^{-1},1)}\left|{E_{W}^{*}(\min(|c|^{-1},1)\times(h\circ g))(X_{n}^{*})-E_{0}(\min(|c|^{-1},1)\times(h\circ g))(X)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\min(|c|^{-1},1)}\sup_{h\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})}|E_{W}^{*}h(X_{n}^{*})-E_{0}h(X)|,

which converges to 0 in outer probability since
X_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}X
by assumption. For the second term on the right-hand side of (
19
), for any
M>0
,

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}Y_{n}^{*}-X_{n}^{*}c|\geq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}Y_{n}^{*}-X_{n}^{*}c|\geq\varepsilon,|X_{n}^{*}|<M)

\displaystyle\qquad+P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}Y_{n}^{*}-X_{n}^{*}c|\geq\varepsilon,|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M)

\displaystyle\leq P_{W}^{*}(|Y_{n}^{*}-c|\geq\varepsilon/M)+P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M).

By assumption,
Y_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}c
so that
P_{W}^{*}(|Y_{n}^{*}-c|\geq\varepsilon/M)=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. Hence, the proof is complete if we can show that given any
\varepsilon,\gamma>0
, there exists
M>0
such that
P_{0}^{*}(P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M)\geq\varepsilon)<\gamma
for all
n
large enough.

For each
\varepsilon,\gamma>0
there exists a sequence of functions
h_{m}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
h_{m}
is
m
-Lipschitz for all
m
,
1\{|x|\geq M\}\leq h_{m}(z)\leq 1
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
m
, and
h_{m}(z)
monotonically decreases to
1\{|x|\geq M\}
as
m\to\infty
for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
. For instance,
h_{m}:x\mapsto\min\{\max(m[|x|-M]+1,0),1\}
satisfies these criteria. For any
\varepsilon>0
and
m\in\{1,2,\dots\}
, we can now write

\displaystyle P^{*}_{W}\left(|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M\right)

\displaystyle=\left[E^{*}_{W}1\left\{|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M\right\}-E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)\right]+\left[E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h_{m}(X)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\left[E_{0}h_{m}(X)-E_{0}1\left\{|X|\geq M\right\}\right]+P_{0}\left(|X|\geq M\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left[E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h_{m}(X)\right]+\left[E_{0}h_{m}(X)-E_{0}1\left\{|X|\geq M\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+P_{0}\left(|X|\geq M\right),

where the second inequality follows because
E^{*}_{W}1\left\{|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M\right\}\leq E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)
for all
\varepsilon>0
and
m\in\{1,2,\dots\}
by assumption. We therefore have

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}(P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M)\geq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}^{*}\left([E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h_{m}(X)]\geq\varepsilon/3\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left([E_{0}h_{m}(X)-E_{0}1\left\{|X|\geq M\right\}]\geq\varepsilon/3\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+P_{0}^{*}(P_{0}\left(|X|\geq M\right)\geq\varepsilon/3)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}^{*}\left([E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h_{m}(X)]\geq\varepsilon/3\right)+1\left\{[E_{0}h_{m}(X)-E_{0}1\left\{|X|\geq M\right\}]\geq\varepsilon/3\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+1\left\{P_{0}\left(|X|\geq M\right)\geq\varepsilon/3\right\}.

(20)

First, since
X
is tight by assumption, we can choose
M
suh that the third term on the right-hand side of (
20
) is 0. Next, for this choice of
M
, by the monotone convergence theorem we can choose
m
large enough so that the second term on the right-hand side of (
20
) is 0. Finally, for these choices of
m
and
M
, for the first term on the right-hand side of (
20
), we have
\min(m^{-1},1)\times h_{m}\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
because
h_{m}
is bounded and
m
-Lipschitz. Therefore, by the assumed conditional weak convergence of
X_{n}^{*}
to
X
in
\mathbb{R}
,
E^{*}_{W}[\min(m^{-1},1)\times h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)]\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}E_{0}[\min(m^{-1},1)\times h_{m}(X)]
for each
m
, so that
E^{*}_{W}h_{m}\left(X_{n}^{*}\right)\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}E_{0}h_{m}(X)
as well. Hence, the first term on the right-hand side of (
20
) converges to 0 for fixed
m
and
\varepsilon
, so we can make it as small as we like for
n
large enough. Hence, for any
\varepsilon
, there exist
M>0
and
m\in\{1,2,\dots\}
such that
P_{W}^{*}(|X_{n}^{*}|\geq M)=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
for
n
large enough.

\hfill\blacksquare

We next have a result that generalizes Theorem 23.4 of
van der Vaart, (2000)
to include smooth bootstraps.

Lemma B.2

(Conditional CLT)
.

Let
\phi_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
satisfy
P_{0}\phi_{0}=0
and
P_{0}\phi_{0}^{2}<\infty
. Let
X_{1},X_{2},\dots
be IID random vectors with mean
\mu
and covariance matrix
\Sigma
. If
\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{0}^{2}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}P_{0}\phi_{0}^{2}
and
(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0
for any
M>0
, then conditionally on every sequence
X_{1},X_{2},\dots
, in outer probabiliy,

n^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n}\right)\phi_{0}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}N(0,P_{0}\phi_{0}^{2}).

Proof of
Lemma

B.2
.

We use the Lindeberg-Feller CLT for triangular arrays (e.g., Proposition 2.27 of
van der Vaart,, 2000
). Since the bootstrap sample
X_{1}^{*},\dotsc,X_{n}^{*}
is drawn IID from
\hat{P}_{n}
given
X_{1},\dotsc,X_{n}
, the conditional mean and variance of
\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})
are given by

\displaystyle E_{W}\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})

\displaystyle=\int\phi_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\hat{P}_{n}(x)=\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{0},\text{ and}

\displaystyle E_{W}[\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})-E_{W}\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})]^{2}

\displaystyle=E_{W}\phi_{0}^{2}(X_{i}^{*})-[E_{W}\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})]^{2}=\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{0}^{2}-(\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{0})^{2}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}P_{0}\phi_{0}^{2}.

We next verify the Lindeberg condition. For any
\varepsilon,\gamma>0
, there exists
M>0
such that
\int\phi_{0}(x)^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}(x)|>M\}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)<\varepsilon/2
and for all
n
large enough,
M\leq\gamma\sqrt{n}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(E_{W}[\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})|>\gamma\sqrt{n}\}]\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}\left(\hat{P}_{n}[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>\gamma\sqrt{n}\}]\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}\left(\hat{P}_{n}[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}\left(|(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]|\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{0}\left(P_{0}[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}\left(|(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]|\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+1\left\{P_{0}[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]\geq\varepsilon/2\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}\left(|(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]|\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\longrightarrow 0

Thus,
E_{W}[\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}(X_{i}^{*})|>\varepsilon\sqrt{n}\}]\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0
. Therefore, the conditions of the Lindeberg-Feller CLT holds conditionally on every sequence
X_{1},X_{2},\dots
, in outer probability, which yields the result.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.9

Proof of
Theorem

3.9
.

We prove the result for the percentile
t
-method; the result for the percentile method follows the same argument setting
\sigma_{n}=\sigma_{n}^{*}=\sigma_{0}=1
(so that the additional conditions for the percentile
t
-method hold automatically).

We first show that
\sigma_{n}^{*-1}(S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\sigma_{n}^{-1}(S_{n}+R_{n})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. By adding and subtracting terms, we note that

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}}{\sigma_{n}^{*}}-\frac{S_{n}+R_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{n}(S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\sigma_{n}(S_{n}+R_{n})+\sigma_{n}(S_{n}+R_{n})-\sigma_{n}^{*}(S_{n}+R_{n})}{\sigma_{n}\sigma_{n}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(S_{n}^{*}-S_{n})+(R_{n}^{*}-R_{n})}{\sigma_{n}^{*}}+\frac{(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{n}^{*})(S_{n}+R_{n})}{\sigma_{n}\sigma_{n}^{*}}.

(21)

Since
\sigma_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\sigma_{0}>0
, we can show that
\sigma_{n}^{*-1}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
using the same logic as the unconditional result. Hence, the above equals
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption.

For any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, by definition of
S_{n}
and
R_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(\frac{T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})}{\sigma_{n}}\leq t\right)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}+n^{1/2}\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]\leq n^{1/2}t\right)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq T_{n}\right),

where
T_{n}:=n^{1/2}t-n^{1/2}\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]
. Thus, by definition of
S_{n}^{*}
and
R_{n}^{*}
,

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(\frac{T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})}{\sigma_{n}^{*}}\leq t\right)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+n^{1/2}\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\left[S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}\right]\leq n^{1/2}t\right)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+n^{1/2}\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\left[S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}\right]\leq T_{n}+n^{1/2}\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+A_{n}^{*}\leq T_{n}\right),

where
A_{n}^{*}:=n^{1/2}\left\{\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\left[S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}\right]-\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]\right\}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(\frac{T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})}{\sigma_{n}^{*}}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\frac{T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})}{\sigma_{n}}\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+A_{n}^{*}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+A_{n}^{*}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|+\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|.

By
Lemma

B.2
, we have
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
. Since
\sigma_{n}^{*2}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\sigma_{0}^{2}
,
\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
by
Lemma

B.1
. Since
A_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
, it follows that
\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+A_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
. Hence, by the Portmanteau theorem (e.g., Lemma 1.3.4 of
van der Vaart & Wellner,, 1996
),

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+A_{n}^{*}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0.

Similarly,
\sigma_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leadsto\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
, so

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq\xi\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|\to 0.

The first statement follows.

By definition of
\xi_{n,p}^{*},

S_{n}^{*}
, and
R_{n}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\xi_{n,p}^{*}:=

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(\frac{T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})}{\sigma_{n}^{*}}\leq\xi\right)\geq p\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(n^{-1/2}\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\left[S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}\right]\leq\xi\right)\geq p\right\}.

Since
\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]
is a function of the original data, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\left(\xi_{n,p}^{*}-\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\inf\left\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(n^{-1/2}\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\left[S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}\right]\leq n^{-1/2}\xi+\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]\right)\geq p\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\inf\left\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(\sigma_{n}^{*-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+A_{n}^{*}\leq\xi\right)\geq p\right\}.

By the above derivations and
van der Vaart,, 2000
, Lemma 21.2
, for any
p\in(0,1)
, we then have

n^{1/2}\left(\xi_{n,p}^{*}-\sigma_{n}^{-1}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]\right)\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\Phi^{-1}(p).

Therefore,

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-\xi_{n,1-\alpha}^{*}\sigma_{n}\leq T(\eta_{0},P_{0})\leq T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-\xi_{n,\beta}^{*}\sigma_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}^{*}\left(n^{1/2}\xi_{n,\beta}^{*}\leq n^{1/2}[T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})]/\sigma_{n}\leq n^{1/2}\xi_{n,1-\alpha}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}^{*}\left(n^{1/2}\left[\xi_{n,\beta}^{*}-\sigma_{n}^{-1}(S_{n}+R_{n})\right]\leq\sigma_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq n^{1/2}\left[\xi_{n,1-\alpha}^{*}-\sigma_{n}^{-1}(S_{n}+R_{n})\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\longrightarrow P_{0}^{*}\left(\Phi^{-1}(\beta)\leq\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq\Phi^{-1}(1-\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=1-\alpha-\beta.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.10

Proof of
Corollary

3.10
.

We note that in the proof of
Theorem

3.9
, the conditions
\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{0}^{2}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}P_{0}\phi_{0}^{2}
and
(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0
were only used to establish that
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
using Lemma
B.2
. However, condition
(B1)
directly implies
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
, so it suffices to check the remainder of the conditions of
Theorem

3.9
. By conditions
(A1)
–
(A3)
, we have
S_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
R_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. By conditions
(B1)
–
(B3)
, we have
S_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
R_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Hence,
S_{n}^{*}-S_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
, which implies the consistency of the bootstrap percentile confidence interval by
Theorem

3.9
. For the percentile
t
interval,
\sigma_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\sigma_{0}
and
\sigma_{n}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\sigma_{0}
by assumption, which also implies
(S_{n}+R_{n})(\sigma_{n}^{*}-\sigma_{n})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.12

Proof of
Theorem

3.12
.

For any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, by definition of
S_{n}
and
R_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(-\left[T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})\right]\leq t\right)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\left(-\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}-n^{1/2}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]\leq n^{1/2}t\right)=P_{0}^{*}\left(-\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq T_{n}\right),

where
T_{n}:=n^{1/2}t+n^{1/2}\left[S_{n}+R_{n}\right]
. Thus, by definition of
S_{n}^{*}
and
R_{n}^{*}
,

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})\leq t\right)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+n^{1/2}\left[S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}+T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})\right]\leq n^{1/2}t\right)

\displaystyle=P_{W}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+B_{n}^{*}\leq T_{n}\right),

where
B_{n}^{*}:=n^{1/2}\left[S_{n}^{*}+S_{n}+R_{n}^{*}+R_{n}+T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})\right]
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(-\left[T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})\right]\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+B_{n}^{*}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(-\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+B_{n}^{*}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}^{*}\left(-\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|.

By
Lemma

B.2
and since
B_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption, we have
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+B_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
. Hence, by the Portmanteau theorem (e.g., Lemma 1.3.4 of
van der Vaart & Wellner,, 1996
),

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{W}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+B_{n}^{*}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0.

Similarly,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leadsto\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
, and since
\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
follows a mean-zero normal distribution,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leadsto-\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
as well. Therefore,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)-P_{0}^{*}\left(-\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq t\right)\right|\to 0.

The first statement follows.

By definition of
\zeta_{n,p}^{*},S_{n}^{*}
and
R_{n}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{n,p}^{*}:=

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(T(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})\leq\zeta\right)\geq p\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(n^{-1/2}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+(S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})+T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\leq\zeta\right)\geq p\right\}.

Since
[S_{n}+R_{n}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]
is a function of the original data, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\left\{\zeta_{n,p}^{*}+[S_{n}+R_{n}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\inf\left\{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(n^{-1/2}\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+(S_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})+T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})\leq n^{-1/2}\zeta-[S_{n}+R_{n}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right)\geq p\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\inf\left\{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}:P_{W}^{*}\left(\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+B_{n}^{*}\leq\zeta\right)\geq p\right\},

As argued above,
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}+B_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
, so by Lemma 21.2 of
van der Vaart, (2000)
, for any
p\in(0,1)
, we then have

n^{1/2}\left\{\zeta_{n,p}^{*}+\left[S_{n}+R_{n}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})\right]\right\}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\sigma_{0}\Phi^{-1}(p).

Therefore,

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(\zeta_{n,1-\alpha}^{*}\leq T(\eta_{0},P_{0})\leq\zeta_{n,\beta}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}^{*}\left(-n^{1/2}[\zeta_{n,\beta}^{*}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\leq n^{1/2}[T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})]\leq-n^{1/2}[\zeta_{n,1-\alpha}^{*}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}^{*}\left(-n^{1/2}[\zeta_{n,\beta}^{*}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\leq\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}+n^{1/2}(S_{n}+R_{n})\leq-n^{1/2}[\zeta_{n,1-\alpha}^{*}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}^{*}\left(-n^{1/2}[\zeta_{n,\beta}^{*}+S_{n}+R_{n}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\leq\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{0}\leq-n^{1/2}[\zeta_{n,1-\alpha}^{*}+S_{n}+R_{n}-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\longrightarrow P_{0}^{*}\left(-\Phi^{-1}(\beta)\leq\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\leq-\Phi^{-1}(1-\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=1-\alpha-\beta,

where the last equality is due to the symmetry of the standard normal distribution. This shows the consistency of Efron’s percentile method.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.13

Proof of
Corollary

3.13
.

As in the proof of Corollary
3.10
, we note that in the proof of
Theorem

3.12
, the conditions
\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{0}^{2}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}P_{0}\phi_{0}^{2}
and
(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})[\phi_{0}^{2}1\{|\phi_{0}|>M\}]\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0
were only used to establish that
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
using Lemma
B.2
. However, condition
(B1)
directly implies
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{0}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
, so it suffices to check the remainder of the conditions of
Theorem

3.12
.

By conditions
(A1)
–
(A3)
, we have
S_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
R_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. By conditions
(B1)
–
(B3)
, we have
S_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
R_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Since
T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption, we then have
S_{n}^{*}+S_{n}+R_{n}^{*}+R_{n}+T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Hence, the result follows by
Theorem

3.12
.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
3.14

Proof of
Theorem

3.14
.

Let
h(x):=x^{2}
. There exists a sequence of functions
h_{m}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
,
m=1,2,\dots
such that
h_{m}\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
for all
m
and
mh_{m}
monotonically increases to
h
as
m\to\infty
. For instance,
h_{m}(x):=\min\{x^{2}/(m\vee 4),1\}
satisfies these criteria. We then write

\displaystyle E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})

\displaystyle=[E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-mE_{W}^{*}h_{m}\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)]+[mE_{W}^{*}h_{m}\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-mE_{0}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+[mE_{0}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})].

Hence, for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-mE_{W}^{*}h_{m}\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}h_{m}\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)}\right|\geq\varepsilon/(3m)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{mE_{0}h_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-mE_{W}^{*}h_{m}\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\left|{E_{W}^{*}g\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}g\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)}\right|\geq\varepsilon/(3m)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+1\left\{E_{0}\left|{mh_{m}\left(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}\right)-h(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right\}.

By conditions
(B1)
-
(B3)
, we have
E_{W}^{*}T_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\overset{P_{0}^{*}}{\leadsto}}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
, i.e.,
\sup_{g\in\mathrm{BL}_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\left|{E_{W}^{*}g\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}g(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})}\right|\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}0
. Therefore, for each fixed
m
, the second term on the right-hand side of previous display converges to 0. By the monotone convergence theorem, the third term on the right-hand side of previous display converges to 0 as
m\longrightarrow\infty
. For the first term on the right hand side of previous display, we note that

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-mE_{W}^{*}h_{m}\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}T_{n}^{*2}-E_{W}^{*}\min\{T_{n}^{*2},m\}}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right)

\displaystyle=P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}(T_{n}^{*2}-m)1\{T_{n}^{*2}\geq m\}}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}T_{n}^{*2}1\{T_{n}^{*2}\geq m\}}\right|\geq\varepsilon/3\right)

\displaystyle\leq E_{0}^{*}E_{W}^{*}T_{n}^{*2}1\{T_{n}^{*2}\geq m\}/(\varepsilon/3),

and
\lim_{m\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}
of this latter expression is 0 by assumption. Therefore, we have

\limsup_{n\to\infty}P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)=\lim_{m\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|{E_{W}^{*}h\left(T_{n}^{*}\right)-E_{0}h(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)=0

for every
\varepsilon>0
. Hence,
\bar{\sigma}_{n}^{2}=E_{W}^{*}T_{n}^{*2}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}E_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0})^{2}=\sigma_{0}^{2}
. By conditions
(A1)
–
(A3)
, we have
n^{1/2}[T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})]\leadsto\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
. By Slutsky’s theorem, we have
n^{1/2}\bar{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}[T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})]\leadsto\sigma_{0}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}
. We then have

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})+z_{\beta}\bar{\sigma}_{n}n^{-1/2}\leq T(\eta_{0},P_{0})\leq T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})+z_{1-\alpha}\bar{\sigma}_{n}n^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=P_{0}\left(-z_{1-\alpha}\leq n^{1/2}[T(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T(\eta_{0},P_{0})]/\bar{\sigma}_{n}\leq-z_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\to P_{0}\left(-z_{1-\alpha}\leq\mathbb{G}_{0}\phi_{0}/\sigma_{0}\leq-z_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=1-\alpha-\beta,

and the result follows.

\hfill\blacksquare

Supplement C
Proof of results in Section
4

We first introduce a Lemma providing conditions under which the (bootstrap) estimating equations-based estimator is (conditionally) consistent, which may be useful in its own right.

Lemma C.1
.

If
\psi_{0}
is a well-separated solution of
\psi\mapsto G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
,
\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class with probability tending to one,
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi,\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}})\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
for all
M>0
, and
\psi_{n}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, then
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. If
\psi_{0}
is a well-separated solution of
\psi\mapsto G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
,
\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class
\mathscr{F}
with conditional probability tending to one,
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi,\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}})\right|=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
for all
M>0
,
\psi_{n}^{*}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
, and
\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, then
\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

By the well-separated assumption, for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0
such that
|P_{0}\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}}|\geq\delta
for every
\psi\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|\psi-\psi_{0}|\geq\varepsilon
. Therefore, the event
\{|\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}|\geq\varepsilon\}
is contained in the event
\{|P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{0}}|\geq\delta\}
. We then write

P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{0}}=-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{0}}\right).

By the assumption that
\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class with probability tending to one,
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, and
\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. Since
\psi_{n}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, for all
\gamma>0
there exists
M
such that
P_{0}^{*}(|\psi_{n}|>M)<\gamma
for all
n
large enough. Thus,

P_{0}\left(\left|P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{0}}\right)\right|\geq\delta\right)\leq P_{0}\left(\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi,\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}}\right)\right|\geq\delta\right)+P_{0}(|\psi_{n}|>M),

which is less than
2\gamma
for all
n
large enough since
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi,\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}})\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. Therefore, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(\left|P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{0}}\right|\geq\delta\right)\longrightarrow 0.

We show that
\psi_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\psi_{0}
using a similar method. By the well-separated condition, the event
\{|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}|\geq\varepsilon\}
is contained in the event
\{|P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{0}}|\geq\delta\}
. We then write

P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{0}}=-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}+\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{0}}\right).

By the assumption that
\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class
\mathscr{F}
with conditional probability tending to one,
(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
and
\left|(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right|\leq\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
, and
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. Since
\psi_{n}^{*}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
, for all
\gamma>0
there exists
M
such that
P_{0}^{*}(P_{W}^{*}(|\psi_{n}^{*}|>M)\geq\gamma/2)=o(1)
. Thus,

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(P_{W}^{*}\left(\left|P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{0}}\right)\right|\geq\delta\right)\geq\gamma\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(P_{W}^{*}\left(\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi,\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}}\right)\right|\geq\delta\right)>\gamma/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+P_{0}^{*}\left(P_{W}^{*}(|\psi_{n}^{*}|>M)>\gamma/2\right),

and both terms are
o(1)
. The result follows.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
4.2

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

We first show that the assumptions imply that
\psi_{n}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\psi_{0}
. By assumption,
\psi_{0}
is a well-separated solution of
\psi\mapsto G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. By condition
(A1)
,
\phi_{n}:=\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Donsker class with probability tending to one, which implies that it is contained in a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class with probability tending to one. By adding and subtracting terms, we can write

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi,\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}}(\psi)-\Gamma_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}+\left[G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right]\left(\psi-\psi_{0}\right)

By assumption,
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}}(\psi)-\Gamma_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
,
P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, and

\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|\left[G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right]\left(\psi-\psi_{0}\right)\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)

for every
M>0
since
\eta_{n}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\eta_{0}
. Finally,
\psi_{n}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. Thus, the conditions of
Lemma

C.1
are satisfied, so
\psi_{n}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\psi_{0}
.

We next show that
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
under the assumptions of the lemma. We have

n^{1/2}(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0})=\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-n^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}+n^{1/2}P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}+n^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}})+(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0})\right].

Now,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by
(A1)
, and
n^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
and
n^{1/2}P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}
are both
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. Since
\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{\mathscr{H}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by assumption, with probability tending to one it holds that

\displaystyle\left|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}})+(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}}(\psi_{n})-G_{0,\eta_{n}}(\psi_{0})+(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}}(\psi_{n})\right|+\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right||\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta:\|\eta-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}<\delta}\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta}(\psi_{n})\right|+\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right|\left|\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}\right|.

Now since
\|\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
and
G_{0,\eta}(\psi_{0})
is continuous in
\eta
at
\eta_{0}
with
G_{0,\eta}(\psi_{0})=-1
,
\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. Since
\psi_{n}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\psi_{0}
as established above, we then have
\sup_{\eta:\|\eta-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}<\delta}\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta}(\psi_{n})\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(|\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}|)
by the assumed differentiability. Hence, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}|\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}|=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)+n^{1/2}o_{P_{0}^{*}}(|\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}|),

which implies that
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

Now since
G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
,
P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
, and
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}

\displaystyle=\left[P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}})-G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\left[G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right]\left(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}\right)+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}}(\psi_{n})+o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2}).

The first term is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by the differentiability assumption since
\|\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
and
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
as discussed above. The result follows.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
4.3

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

We first show that the assumptions imply that
\psi_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\psi_{0}
. By assumption,
\psi_{0}
is a well-separated solution of
\psi\mapsto G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. By condition
(A1)
,
\phi_{n}^{*}:=\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Donsker class with probability tending to one, which implies that it is contained in a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class with probability tending to one. Adding and subtracting terms, we can write

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(\phi_{\psi,\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi,\eta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi)-\Gamma_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}+\left[G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right]\left(\psi-\psi_{0}\right)

By assumption,
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi)-\Gamma_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi_{0})\right|=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
,
P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, and

\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|\left[G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right]\left(\psi-\psi_{0}\right)\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)

for every
M>0
since
\eta_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\eta_{0}
. Finally,
\psi_{n}^{*}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
and
\|\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by condition
(A1)
. Thus, the conditions of
Lemma

C.1
are satisfied, so
\psi_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\psi_{0}
.

We next show that
|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}})+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})|=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}|)
under the assumptions of the lemma. Since
\|\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption, with conditional probability tending to one it holds that

\displaystyle\left|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}})+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*})-G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi_{0})+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*})\right|+\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right||\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta:\|\eta-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}<\delta}\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta}(\psi_{n}^{*})\right|+\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right|\left|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}\right|.

Now since
\|\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
and
G_{0,\eta}(\psi_{0})
is continuous in
\eta
at
\eta_{0}
with
G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi_{0})=-1
,
\left|G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right|=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
. Since
\psi_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\psi_{0}
as shown above, we also have
\sup_{\eta:\|\eta-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}<\delta}\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta}(\psi_{n}^{*})\right|=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}|)
. The result follows.

We now show that
\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
under the assumptions of the lemma. We have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}+\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-n^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}+n^{1/2}P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}})+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})\right].

Now,
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by
(B1)
,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by
(A1)
, and
n^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
and
n^{1/2}P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}
are both
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. We therefore have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}|

\displaystyle=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)+n^{1/2}\left|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}})+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})\right|

\displaystyle=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)+n^{1/2}o_{P_{W}^{*}}(|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}|),

which implies that
\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

We now show the first statement of
Lemma

4.3
. We write

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{n}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}&=[\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}]-[\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}]\\
&\qquad+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}})+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}.\end{split}

(22)

By
Lemma

4.2
, we have
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. By the definitions of
\psi_{n}
and
\psi_{n}^{*}
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. By conditions
(A1)
and
(A2)
and Lemma 19.24 of
van der Vaart, (2000)
, we have
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{0}})=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Similarly, by
(A1)
(b),
(B1)
(a),
(B2)
and a minor modification of Lemma 19.24
van der Vaart, (2000)
, we have
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{0}})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Hence,
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Finally, since
G_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
,
P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
, and
\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
, we have

\displaystyle|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|P_{0}(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}})+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})\right|+\left|{P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}}\right|

\displaystyle=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}|)+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2}).

The result follows.

We next show the second statement in
Lemma

4.3
. Since
R_{n}=\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
and
R_{n}^{*}=\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{n}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
, by adding and subtracting terms, we have

\displaystyle R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right)-2\left(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}-2P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}})+\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0}\right)\left(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}\right)+\left(2\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right).

As we showed above,
\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. By assumption,
P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}-2P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. By the definitions of
\psi_{n}^{*}
and
\psi_{n}
,
2\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
are both
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}

\displaystyle=-2\left(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}\right)+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=-2\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}}(\psi_{n})-2\left[G_{0,\eta_{n}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})+1\right](\psi_{n}-\psi_{0})+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2}).

The first two terms are
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
as discussed above. The result follows.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
4.4

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

By the same argument as used in
Lemma

4.3
, and since
\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption, we can show that the conditions of
Lemma

C.1
are satisfied, so that
\psi_{n}^{*}\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}\psi_{0}
.

We recall that
\hat{\psi}_{n}:=T(\hat{\eta}_{n},\hat{P}_{n})
. By the same argument as used in
Lemma

4.3
,
P_{0}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{0}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
implies that
\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. We write

P_{0}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{0}}=\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}-\left(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\right)\left(\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}\right)-P_{0}\left(\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}-\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{0}}\right).

By the definition of
\hat{\psi}_{n}
, we have
\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}=0
. Under the assumptions
\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
and
\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}
is contained in
\mathscr{F}
with probability tending to one, we have
(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. Finally, since
\hat{\psi}_{n}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
and
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|\Gamma_{0,\hat{\eta}_{n}}(\psi)-\Gamma_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
for every
M>0
,
P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\hat{\eta}_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, and
\|\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta_{0}\|_{\mathscr{H}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, by the same argument as in
Lemma

4.2
,
P_{0}\left(\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}-\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{0}}\right)=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. Thus,
P_{0}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{0}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, so that
\hat{\psi}_{n}\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}\psi_{0}
. Hence, we also have
\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}=(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0})-(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0})\underset{W}{\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}}0
.

We now show that
\hat{P}_{n}\left(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right)+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})
under the assumptions of the lemma. With conditional probability tending to one, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|{\hat{P}_{n}\big(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}\big)+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|{\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*})-\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\hat{\psi}_{n})+(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left|{\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*})-\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\hat{\psi}_{n})-\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\hat{\psi}_{n})(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})}\right|+\left|{\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\hat{\psi}_{n})+1}\right|\left|{\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{\eta:\left\|{\eta-\hat{\eta}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathscr{H}}<\delta}\left|{\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\eta}(\psi_{n}^{*})}\right|+\left|{\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\hat{\psi}_{n})+1}\right|\left|{\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}}\right|

Since by assumption
\hat{G}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\psi_{n})+1=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
, the second term on the right hand side of previous display is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})
. The result follows.

We next show that
\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
under the assumptions of the lemma. We have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-n^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}+n^{1/2}\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}+n^{1/2}\left[\hat{P}_{n}\left(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right)+\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}\right].

Now,
\mathbb{G}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
because
(B1)
holds for
\phi_{n}^{*}=\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
by assumption, and
n^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
and
n^{1/2}\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}
are both
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. As discussed above,
\hat{P}_{n}\left(\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right)+\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}|\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}|=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)+n^{1/2}o_{P_{W}^{*}}(|\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}|),

which implies that
\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

We can now show the first statement of
Lemma

4.4
. We recall that
\psi_{n}^{\circ}:=T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})
. We can write

\displaystyle R_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{n}^{\circ}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}+\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right)-\left(\psi_{n}^{\circ}-\hat{\psi}_{n}\right)+\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}};

We have
\psi_{n}^{\circ}-\hat{\psi}_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by definition of
\psi_{n}^{*}
, and

\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}+\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}\left(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}\right)=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})

by the derivations above. Hence,
R_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

Lastly, we show the second statement in
Lemma

4.4
. Recall that
R_{n}=\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
and
R_{n}^{*}=\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{n}^{\circ}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}
. By adding and subtracting terms, we can write

\displaystyle R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}+\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right)-\left(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}\right)+\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}\right)+\left(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{n}^{\circ}\right).

We have
\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n}+\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}-\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by the derivations above,
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

4.2
,
\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{n}^{\circ}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}-P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption, and
\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\eta_{n}^{*}}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by the definitions of
\psi_{n}
and
\psi_{n}^{*}
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Supplement D
Proof of results in Section
5

See
5.1

Proof of
Proposition

5.1
.

We use
Theorem

3.1
. Condition
(A1)
holds because
\eta_{n}
falls in a
P_{0}
-Donsker class by assumption. For any
\varepsilon>0
, by the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}^{*}\left(\left\|{\phi_{n}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(2\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(2\left|{\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\int\eta_{0}^{2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(2M\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(2\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\left\|{\eta_{n}+\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{0}^{*}\left(2M\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(4M\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{0}^{*}\left(\left\|{\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq M\right).

Each term on the right-hand side converges to 0, which implies condition
(A2)
.

For condition
(A3)
for
\psi_{n,1}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{1}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T_{1}(\eta_{0},P_{0})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\left(2\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}-\int\eta_{n}^{2}\right)-\int\eta_{0}^{2}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\eta_{n}

\displaystyle=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}

which is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{1/2})
by assumption. For
\psi_{n,2}
and
\psi_{n,3}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{2}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T_{2}(\eta_{0},P_{0})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\int\eta_{0}^{2}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\eta_{n}

\displaystyle=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}+2\left[\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}\right]

\displaystyle T_{3}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T_{3}(\eta_{0},P_{0})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}-P_{0}\eta_{0}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\eta_{n}

\displaystyle=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}+\left[\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}\right],

which are both
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
when
\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/4})
.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
5.2

Proof of
Proposition

5.2
.

We use
Theorem

3.4
. Condition
(B1)
holds by
Lemma

3.6
because
\eta_{n}^{*}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Donsker class with
P_{W}^{*}
probability tending to one. For any
\varepsilon>0
, by the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities, we have

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(\left\|{\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(2\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(2\left|{\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\int\eta_{0}^{2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(2M\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(2\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}+\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(2M\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(4M\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq M\right),

Each term on the right-hand side is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, which implies condition
(B2)
.

For condition
(B3)
for
\psi_{n,1}^{*}
, we write

\displaystyle\left|{T_{1}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T_{1}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|{\left(2\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}-\int\eta_{n}^{*2}\right)-\left(2\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}-\int\eta_{n}^{2}\right)-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})2\eta_{n}^{*}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|{-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}-2\int\eta_{n}(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})+2(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})+2P_{0}(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|{-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}-2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})+2(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}+2\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}+2n^{-1/2}\left|{\mathbb{G}_{n}(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})}\right|.

The first two terms are
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. In addition,
\mathbb{G}_{n}(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
because
\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
and
\eta_{n}^{*}
is contained in a
P_{0}
-Donsker class with
P_{W}^{*}
-probability tending to one. Hence, condition
(B3)
holds for
\psi_{n,1}^{*}
, and the conditional asymptotic linearity result for
\psi_{n,1}^{*}
follows.

For condition
(B3)
for
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
and
\psi_{n,3}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{2}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T_{2}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\int\eta_{n}^{2}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\eta_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}+2\left(\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}\right)-2\left(\int\eta_{n}\eta_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle T_{3}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T_{3}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\eta_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}+\left(\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}\right)+\left(\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\left(\int\eta_{n}\eta_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}^{*}\right).

Now,
\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}
and
\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}
are both
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption, and
\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. In addition,

\displaystyle\left|{\int\eta_{n}\eta_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}^{*}}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|{\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})+\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\eta_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}+n^{-1/2}\left|{\mathbb{G}_{n}(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})}\right|+\left|\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\eta_{n}\right|,

which is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Therefore, condition
(B3)
holds for
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
and
\psi_{n,3}^{*}
.

We now turn to the setting where
\eta_{n}
and
\eta_{n}^{*}
are kernel density estimators using the same bandwidth
h
. We note that for any
x
, since
\hat{P}_{n}
is the empirical bootstrap,

\displaystyle E_{W}^{*}\left[\eta_{n}^{*}(x)\right]

\displaystyle=E_{W}^{*}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}h^{-d}K\left(\frac{X_{i}^{*}-x}{h}\right)\right]=\mathbb{P}_{n}h^{-d}K\left(\frac{X_{i}-x}{h}\right)=\eta_{n}(x).

We therefore have

\displaystyle E_{W}^{*}\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int E_{W}^{*}[\eta_{n}^{*}(x)-\eta_{n}(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int\mathrm{Var}_{W}^{*}(\eta_{n}^{*}(x))\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\frac{1}{nh^{2d}}\mathrm{Var}_{W}^{*}\left[K\left(\frac{X_{1}^{*}-x}{h}\right)\right]\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{nh^{2d}}\int E_{W}^{*}\left[K\left(\frac{X_{1}^{*}-x}{h}\right)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nh^{2d}}\int\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[K\left(\frac{X_{i}-x}{h}\right)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nh^{d}}\int K^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Hence, by Chebyshev’s inequality, for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(n^{1/4}\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{1/2}E_{W}^{*}\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}/\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(nh^{2d}\right)^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{-2}\int K^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

which goes to zero since
nh^{2d}\longrightarrow\infty
. Therefore
\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/4})
.

\hfill\blacksquare

The proof of
Proposition

5.3
relies on technical lemmas that are stated and proved in Appendix
E
.
