Since
\phi_{P}=2(\eta_{P}-\psi_{P})
, we then have

S_{n}^{*}=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0})=2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})
and
S_{n}=2(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})
. By

E.7
, we have
S_{n}^{*}-S_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
under the stated assumptions.

We have
R_{n}^{*}=\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{n}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}=\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{n}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\eta_{n}^{*}
. For the one-step estimator
\psi_{n,1}^{*}

R_{n}^{*}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}+\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n})
and
R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}
. Hence

R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}+2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n}).

If

and

E.7
. This implies bootstrap percentile confidence intervals based on
\psi_{n,1}^{*}
are asymptotically valid by
Theorem

3.9
.

For the plug-in estimator
\psi_{n,2}^{*}

R_{n}^{*}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}+\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n})
and
R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}
. Hence,

R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}+2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}+2\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n}).

Since
nh^{2m}\longrightarrow 0
and
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
hold, we have
\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}-2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by

E.8
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}-2\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

E.9
and
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

E.7
. This implies bootstrap percentile confidence intervals based on
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
are asymptotically valid by
Theorem

3.9
.

For the empirical mean plug-in estimator
\psi_{n,3}^{*}
, we have
R_{n}^{*}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}+\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}/2+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}/2+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n})
and
R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}/2
. Hence,

R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}+2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}/2+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n}).

Since
nh^{2m}\longrightarrow 0
and
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
hold, we have
\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}=2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

E.8
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}=2\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

E.9
and
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

E.7
. This implies bootstrap percentile confidence intervals based on
\psi_{n,3}^{*}
are asymptotically valid by
Theorem

3.9
.

\hfill\blacksquare

See

Proof of Proposition

.

We use
Theorem

3.4
. Condition
(B1)
holds because
P_{W}(\eta_{n}^{*}\in\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{P_{0}^{*}}1
and the conditions of
Lemma

3.7
hold by assumption. For any
\varepsilon>0
, by the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities, we have

\displaystyle P_{W}^{*}\left(\left\|{\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(2\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(2\left|{\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\int\eta_{0}^{2}}\right|\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(2M\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(2\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}+\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq P_{W}^{*}\left(2M\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(4M\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq\varepsilon/2\right)+P_{W}^{*}\left(\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\geq M\right).

Since
\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\leq\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}+\left\|{\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}
and
\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}\leq\left\|{\eta_{n}^{*}}\right\|_{\infty}
, each term on the right-hand side of the previous display is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, which implies condition
(B2)
.

For condition
(B3)
, we have

\displaystyle T_{1}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T_{1}(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}=

\displaystyle-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n})^{2}+\int(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta_{n})^{2},

which is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. Hence, condition
(B3)
holds for
\psi_{n,1}^{*}
. We next have

\displaystyle T_{2}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T_{2}(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\int\eta_{n}^{2}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\eta_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n})^{2}+\int(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta_{n})^{2}+2\left[\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-2\left[\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\hat{P}_{n}\eta_{n}\right],

\displaystyle T_{3}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})-T_{3}(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n}\eta_{n}-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\eta_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n})^{2}+\int(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta_{n})^{2}+\left[\int\eta_{n}^{*2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[\int\eta_{n}^{2}-\hat{P}_{n}\eta_{n}\right],

both of which are
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. Hence, condition
(B3)
holds for
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
and
\psi_{n,3}^{*}
.

The proof of

relies on technical lemmas that are stated and proved in Appendix

.
See

Proof of
Proposition

5.5
.

Since
\phi_{P}=2(\eta_{P}-\psi_{P})
, we then have

S_{n}^{*}=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0})=2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})
and
S_{n}=2(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})
. By
Lemma

F.8
, we have
S_{n}^{*}-S_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
under the stated assumptions.

We have
R_{n}^{*}=\psi_{n}^{*}-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\phi_{n}^{*}=\psi_{n}^{*}-T(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})-2(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\hat{P}_{n})\eta_{n}^{*}
. For the one-step estimator
\psi_{n,1}^{*}
, we have
R_{n}^{*}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}
and
R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}
. Hence

R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}+\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}

If
nh^{4m}\longrightarrow 0
and
nh^{2d}\longrightarrow\infty
hold, then
\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

F.9
. This implies bootstrap percentile confidence intervals based on
\psi_{n,1}^{*}
are asymptotically valid by
Theorem

3.9
.

For the plug-in estimator
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
, we have
R_{n}^{*}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}
and
R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}
. Hence,

R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}+\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}.

Since
nh^{4m}\longrightarrow 0
and
nh^{2d}\longrightarrow\infty
hold, we have
\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

F.9
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

F.10
. This implies bootstrap percentile confidence intervals based on
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
are asymptotically valid by
Theorem

3.9
.

For the empirical mean plug-in estimator
\psi_{n,3}^{*}
, we have
R_{n}^{*}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}/2
and
R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}/2
. Hence,

R_{n}^{*}-R_{n}=-\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}+\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}/2+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}/2.

Since
nh^{4m}\longrightarrow 0
and
nh^{2d}\longrightarrow\infty
hold, we have
\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})^{2}-\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by

F.9
,
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Lemma

F.10
. This implies bootstrap percentile confidence intervals based on
\psi_{n,3}^{*}
are asymptotically valid by
Theorem

3.9
.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
5.6

Proof of
Proposition

5.6
.

Condition
(A1)
holds by the Donsker assumptions, the boundedness assumptions on
\mu_{n}
and
g_{n}
, and
E(Y^{2})<\infty
together with preservation of the Donsker condition under Lipschitz transformations (e.g., Theorem 2.10.6 of
van der Vaart & Wellner,, 1996
).

We next address condition
(A2)
. By adding and subtracting terms, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{n}(y,a,z)-\phi_{0}(y,a,z)

\displaystyle=(1-a)\left[y-\mu_{0}(z)\right]\frac{g_{n}(z)}{1-g_{n}(z)}\frac{1}{\pi_{n}\pi_{0}}\left[\pi_{0}-\pi_{n}\right]+a\left[\mu_{0}(z)-\psi_{0}\right]\frac{1}{\pi_{n}\pi_{0}}\left[\pi_{0}-\pi_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(1-a)\left[y-\mu_{0}(z)\right]}{\pi_{0}\left[1-g_{n}(z)\right]\left[1-g_{0}(z)\right]}\left[g_{n}(z)-g_{0}(z)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi_{n}}\left[\frac{a}{g_{n}(z)}-1\right]\frac{g_{n}(z)}{1-g_{n}(z)}\left[\mu_{n}(z)-\mu_{0}(z)\right]-\frac{a}{\pi_{n}}\left[\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}\right].

Therefore, by the assumed bounds and the triangle inequality,
\left\|{\phi_{n}-\phi_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}
is bounded up to a constant by

\displaystyle|\pi_{n}-\pi_{0}|+\left\|{g_{n}-g_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+\left\|{\mu_{n}-\mu_{0}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+|\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}|.

We note that consistency of
\mu_{n}
and
g_{n}
implies consistency of
\pi_{n}
and
\psi_{n}
. Therefore, condition
(A2)
holds.

For condition
(A3)
for the one-step estimator
\psi_{n,1}=T_{1}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})
, we have
T_{1}(\eta_{n},\mathbb{P}_{n})-T_{1}(\eta_{0},P_{0})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\phi_{n}=\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{n}
. Using the law of total expectation, a straightforward calculation shows that

\displaystyle P_{0}\phi_{\psi,\eta}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{(g_{0}-g)(\mu-\mu_{0})}{\pi(1-g)}\right\}+\frac{\pi_{0}}{\pi}(\psi_{0}-\psi).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{n}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{(g_{0}-g_{n})(\mu_{n}-\mu_{0})}{\pi_{n}(1-g_{n})}\right\}+\frac{\pi_{n}-\pi_{0}}{\pi_{n}}(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}).

These terms are both
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by the assumed rates and boundedness conditions. The result for the one-step estimator follows.

For the estimating equations-based estimator, we use
Lemma

4.2
. We have already established that condition
(A1)
holds for
\phi_{n}=\phi_{\psi_{n},\eta_{n}}
. We have that the population estimating equation is
G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)=\psi-\psi_{0}
, so
G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi_{0})=0
and
\inf_{|\psi-\psi_{0}|>\delta}|G_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)|=\delta
, and hence
\psi_{0}
is a well-separated solution of the population estimating equation. Furthermore, it is straightforward to see that the boundedness conditions and the assumption that
E_{0}(Y^{2})<\infty
imply that
\psi_{n,2}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. Next, defining
G_{0,\eta}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})=-\pi_{0}\pi^{-1}
, we then have
G_{0,\eta_{0}}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})=-1
and
\lim_{\eta\to\eta_{0}}G_{0,\eta}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})=\lim_{\eta\to\eta_{0}}-\pi_{0}\pi^{-1}=-1
. In addition,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0,\eta}(\psi)

\displaystyle=G_{0,\eta}(\psi)-G_{0,\eta}(\psi_{0})-G_{0,\eta}^{\prime}(\psi_{0})(\psi-\psi_{0})

\displaystyle=P_{0}\phi_{\eta,\psi}-P_{0}\phi_{\eta,\psi_{0}}+\frac{\pi_{0}}{\pi}(\psi-\psi_{0})

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{g_{0}(Z)-g(Z)}{\pi\left[1-g(Z)\right]}\left[\mu(Z)-\mu_{0}(Z)\right]\right\}+\frac{\pi_{0}}{\pi}(\psi-\psi_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad-P_{0}\left\{\frac{g_{0}(Z)-g(Z)}{\pi\left[1-g(Z)\right]}\left[\mu(Z)-\mu_{0}(Z)\right]\right\}-\frac{\pi_{0}}{\pi}(\psi_{0}-\psi_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\pi_{0}}{\pi}(\psi_{0}-\psi)

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore, both conditions about
\Gamma_{0,\eta}
hold. Finally,

\displaystyle P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{g_{0}-g_{n}}{\pi_{n}\left[1-g_{n}\right]}\left[\mu_{n}-\mu_{0}\right]\right\}-\frac{\pi_{0}}{\pi_{n}}(\psi_{0}-\psi_{0})

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{g_{0}-g_{n}}{\pi_{n}\left[1-g_{n}\right]}\left[\mu_{n}-\mu_{0}\right]\right\},

which is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. Hence, condition
(A3)
holds for the estimating equations-based estimator.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
5.7

Proof of
Proposition

5.7
.

By
Lemma

3.6
, condition
(B1)
holds by the Donsker assumptions, the boundedness assumptions on
\mu_{n}^{*}
and
g_{n}^{*}
, and
E(Y^{2})<\infty
together with preservation of the Donsker condition under Lipschitz transformations.

As in the proof of
Proposition

5.6
, we can show that
\left\|\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}
is bounded up to a constant by

\left|\pi_{n}^{*}-\pi_{0}\right|+\left\|g_{n}^{*}-g_{0}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+\left\|\mu_{n}^{*}-\mu_{0}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+\left|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}\right|.

Therefore, condition
(B2)
holds under the conditional weak
L_{2}(P_{0})
conditional consistency of
g_{n}^{*}
and
\mu_{n}^{*}
, which also implies weak conditional consistency of
\psi_{n}^{*}
and
\pi_{n}^{*}
.

For condition
(B3)
for the one-step estimator
\psi_{n,1}^{*}=T_{1}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})
, as in equation (
8
), we have

\displaystyle R_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\left[\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{n}^{*}\right]-\left[\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{n}\right]+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n})

By the proof of
Proposition

5.6
,
\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
, and

n^{1/2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{n})=\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}\right)-\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\phi_{n}-\phi_{0}\right),

which is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Proposition

5.6
and the above. By the derivations in
Proposition

5.6
, we also have

\displaystyle\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}+P_{0}\phi_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{\left(g_{0}-g_{n}^{*}\right)\left(\mu_{n}^{*}-\mu_{0}\right)}{\pi_{n}^{*}\left(1-g_{n}^{*}\right)}\right\}+\frac{\pi_{n}^{*}-\pi_{0}}{\pi_{n}^{*}}\left(\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}\right).

This is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption.

For condition
(B3)
for the estimating equations-based estimator
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
, we use
Lemma

4.3
. We note that the conditions of
Lemma

4.2
hold by
Proposition

5.6
and condition
(B1)
holds by the above. We have
\psi_{n,2}^{*}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by the boundedness conditions and because
E_{0}(Y^{2})<\infty
. As in the proof of
Proposition

5.6
, we have
\Gamma_{0,\eta}(\psi)=0
for all
\eta\in\mathscr{H}
and
\psi\in\mathbb{R}
, so that
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}\left|\Gamma_{0,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi)-\Gamma_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi_{0})\right|=0
for every
M>0
. Finally,

\displaystyle P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\eta_{n}^{*}}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{\left(g_{0}-g_{n}^{*}\right)\left(\mu_{n}-\mu_{0}\right)}{\pi_{n}^{*}\left(1-g_{n}^{*}\right)}\right\}.

which is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption.

\hfill\blacksquare

See
5.8

Proof of
Proposition

5.8
.

We use
Lemma

3.7
to show condition
(B1)
holds. By the assumed classes of
\mu_{n}^{*}
and
g_{n}^{*}
, with conditional probability tending to 1,
\phi_{n}^{*}
falls in a class
\mathscr{F}
with envelope
F(y,a,z):=c|Y|+d
for some fixed
c,d\in(0,\infty)
. Thus, condition (i) of
Lemma

3.7
holds by the assumption that
\lim_{M\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathscr{P}}E_{P}[Y^{2}I(Y^{2}>M)]=0
. Next, by preservation of the finite uniform entropy integral condition under Lipschitz transformations, (see, e.g., Lemma 9.17 of
Kosorok,, 2008
),
\mathscr{F}
posesses finite uniform entropy integral. Hence, condition (ii) of
Lemma

3.7
is satisfied by Theorem 2.8.3 of
van der Vaart & Wellner, (1996)
. Next, as noted following
Lemma

3.7
,
\sup_{f,g\in\mathscr{F}}\left|(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})(fg)\right|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
implies (
2
). Let
\mu,\bar{\mu}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mu}
and
g,\bar{g}\in\mathscr{F}_{g}
, and let
\pi,\bar{\pi}
be implied by
g,\bar{g}
and
\psi,\bar{\psi}
be implied by
\mu,g
and
\bar{\mu},\bar{g}
.
Using the fact that
a(1-a)=0
and the tower property, we then have

\displaystyle(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left(\phi_{\mu,g}\phi_{\bar{\mu},\bar{g}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\int\left[\frac{(1-a)g\bar{g}}{\pi\bar{\pi}(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}(y-\mu)(y-\bar{\mu})+\frac{a}{\pi\bar{\pi}}(\mu-\psi)(\bar{\mu}-\bar{\psi})\right]\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad=\int\left[\frac{(1-a)g\bar{g}}{\pi\bar{\pi}(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}\left\{y^{2}-(\mu+\bar{\mu})y+\mu\bar{\mu}\right\}\right]\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int\left[\frac{a}{\pi\bar{\pi}}(\mu-\psi)(\bar{\mu}-\bar{\psi})\right]\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\pi\bar{\pi}}\int\frac{g\bar{g}}{(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}\left\{(1-\hat{g}_{n})\left[(\hat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}+\hat{\mu}_{n}^{2})-(\mu+\bar{\mu})\hat{\mu}_{n}+\mu\bar{\mu}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\hat{Q}_{n}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(1-g_{0})\left[(\sigma_{0}^{2}+\mu_{0}^{2})-(\mu+\bar{\mu})\mu_{0}+\mu\bar{\mu}\right]\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi\bar{\pi}}\int(\mu-\psi)(\bar{\mu}-\bar{\psi})\left[\hat{g}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{Q}_{n}-g_{0}\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\pi\bar{\pi}}\int\frac{g\bar{g}}{(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}\left\{(1-\hat{g}_{n})\left[(\hat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}+\hat{\mu}_{n}^{2})-(\mu+\bar{\mu})\hat{\mu}_{n}+\mu\bar{\mu}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-(1-g_{0})\left[(\sigma_{0}^{2}+\mu_{0}^{2})-(\mu+\bar{\mu})\mu_{0}+\mu\bar{\mu}\right]\right\}\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi\bar{\pi}}\int\frac{g\bar{g}(1-\hat{g}_{n})}{(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}\left[(\hat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}+\hat{\mu}_{n}^{2})-(\mu+\bar{\mu})\hat{\mu}_{n}+\mu\bar{\mu}\right]\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi\bar{\pi}}\int(\mu-\psi)(\bar{\mu}-\bar{\psi})(\hat{g}_{n}-g_{0})\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}+\frac{1}{\pi\bar{\pi}}\int(\mu-\psi)(\bar{\mu}-\bar{\psi})\hat{g}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0}).

By adding and subtracting terms and using the assumed bounds, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mu,\bar{\mu},g,\bar{g}}\left|(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left(\phi_{\mu,g}\phi_{\bar{\mu},\bar{g}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\hat{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{L_{2}(Q_{0})}+\|\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{0}\|_{L_{2}(Q_{0})}+\|\hat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\|_{L_{2}(Q_{0})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{g,\bar{g}}\left|\int\frac{g\bar{g}(1-\hat{g}_{n})}{(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}(\hat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}+\hat{\mu}_{n}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{g,\bar{g},\mu}\left|\int\frac{g\bar{g}(1-\hat{g}_{n})}{(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}\mu\hat{\mu}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{g,\bar{g},\mu,\bar{\mu}}\left|\int\frac{g\bar{g}(1-\hat{g}_{n})}{(1-g)(1-\bar{g})}\mu\bar{\mu}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{\mu}\left|\int\mu\hat{g}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})\right|+\sup_{\mu,\bar{\mu}}\left|\int\mu\bar{\mu}\hat{g}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})\right|.

Since
\hat{\mu}_{n}\in\mathscr{F}_{\mu}
with probability tending to one, by assumption, each of these terms is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
, which implies (
2
). Hence, the conditions of
Lemma

3.7
hold, which implies condition
(B1)
.

We next show condition
(B2)
holds. As in the proof of
Proposition

5.6
, we can show that
\left\|\phi_{n}^{*}-\phi_{0}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}
is bounded up to a constant by

\displaystyle\left|\pi_{n}^{*}-\pi_{0}\right|+\left\|g_{n}^{*}-g_{0}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+\left\|\mu_{n}^{*}-\mu_{0}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}+\left|\psi_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}\right|.

Each of these terms is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
.

Finally, we turn to condition
(B3)
. By the tower property, we can show that

P\phi_{\mu,g,\psi}=Q_{P}\left[\frac{(g_{P}-g)(\mu-\mu_{P})}{(1-g)\pi}\right]+\frac{\pi_{P}}{\pi}\left[\psi(P)-\psi\right]

for
\pi:=\int g\,dQ
. Thus,

\psi(\eta)-\psi(P)+P\phi_{\eta}=Q_{P}\left[\frac{(g_{P}-g)(\mu-\mu_{P})}{(1-g)\pi(\eta)}\right]+\frac{\pi(\eta)-\pi_{P}}{\pi(\eta)}\left[\psi(\eta)-\psi(P)\right].

Hence, for the one-step estimator
\psi_{n,1}^{*}=T_{1}(\eta_{n}^{*},\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})
, as in equation (
8
), we have

\displaystyle R_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\left[\psi(\eta_{n}^{*})-\psi(\hat{\eta}_{n})+\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{n}^{*}\right]-\left[\psi(\eta_{n})-\psi(\hat{\eta}_{n})+\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\hat{Q}_{n}\left\{\frac{(\hat{g}_{n}-g_{n}^{*})(\mu_{n}^{*}-\hat{\mu}_{n})}{\pi_{n}^{*}(1-g_{n}^{*})}\right\}+\frac{\pi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\pi}_{n}}{\pi_{n}^{*}}(\psi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\left[\hat{Q}_{n}\left\{\frac{(\hat{g}_{n}-g_{n})(\mu_{n}-\hat{\mu}_{n})}{\pi_{n}(1-g_{n})}\right\}+\frac{\pi_{n}-\hat{\pi}_{n}}{\pi_{n}}(\psi_{n}-\hat{\psi}_{n})\right].

Each term is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. The result for the one-step estimator follows.

For condition
(B3)
for the estimating equations-based estimator
\psi_{n,2}^{*}
, we use
Lemma

4.4
. Since the conditions of
Proposition

5.6
hold, the conditions of
Lemma

4.2
hold as well. We have
\psi_{n,2}^{*}=O_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
by the boundedness conditions on
\mu_{n}^{*}
,
g_{n}^{*}
, and
\hat{g}_{n}
, and the moment condition for
\hat{P}_{n}
. A similar argument shows that
\hat{\psi}_{n}=O_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
. As in the proof of
Proposition

5.6
, we have
\Gamma_{0,\eta}(\psi)=0
for any
\eta\in\mathscr{H}
and
\psi\in\mathbb{R}
, so that the condition
\sup_{|\psi|\leq M}|\Gamma_{0,\hat{\eta}_{n}}(\psi)-\Gamma_{0,\eta_{0}}(\psi)|=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
holds. Next, defining
\hat{G}_{n,\eta}^{\prime}(\hat{\psi}_{n})=-\hat{\pi}_{n}\pi^{-1}
, we then have
\hat{G}_{n,\hat{\eta}_{n}^{*}}^{\prime}(\hat{\psi}_{n})+1=1-\hat{\pi}_{n}/\pi_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
because
\pi_{n}^{*}-\hat{\pi}_{n}=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(1)
and
g_{n}^{*}
is bounded away from zero. In addition,

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\eta}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\hat{G}_{n,\eta}(\psi)-\hat{G}_{n,\eta}(\hat{\psi}_{n})-\hat{G}_{n,\eta}^{\prime}(\hat{\psi}_{n})(\psi-\hat{\psi}_{n})

\displaystyle=\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\eta,\psi}-\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\eta,\hat{\psi}_{n}}+\frac{\hat{\pi}_{n}}{\pi}(\psi-\hat{\psi}_{n})

\displaystyle=\hat{P}_{n}\left\{\frac{\hat{g}_{n}-g}{\pi\left[1-g\right]}\left[\mu-\hat{\mu}_{n}\right]\right\}+\frac{\hat{\pi}_{n}}{\pi}(\psi-\hat{\psi}_{n})-\hat{P}_{n}\left\{\frac{\hat{g}_{n}-g}{\pi\left[1-g\right]}\left[\mu-\hat{\mu}_{n}\right]\right\}-\frac{\hat{\pi}_{n}}{\pi}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\hat{\psi}_{n})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\hat{\pi}_{n}}{\pi}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi)

\displaystyle=0.

Hence, the conditions about
|\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\eta_{n}^{*}}(\psi)-\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\hat{\eta}_{n}}(\hat{\psi})|
and
|\hat{\Gamma}_{n,\eta}(\psi)|
hold. Next,
\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\hat{\eta}_{n}}
and
\phi_{\psi_{n}^{*},\hat{\eta}_{n}^{*}}
fall in a
P_{0}
-Glivenko Cantelli class with probability to tending one by the finite entropy integral and boundedness assumptions. Next, we verify that
\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
holds under the stated assumptions.

\displaystyle\int\phi_{\mu,g}(y,a,w)\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle=\int\left[\frac{(1-a)g}{\pi(1-g)}(y-\mu)+\frac{a}{\pi}(\mu-\psi)\right]\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int\frac{g}{(1-g)}\left\{(1-\hat{g}_{n})(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu)\,\mathrm{d}\hat{Q}_{n}-(1-g_{0})(\mu_{0}-\mu)\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi}\int(\mu-\psi)\left[\hat{g}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{Q}_{n}-g_{0}\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int\frac{g}{(1-g)}\left\{(1-\hat{g}_{n})(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu)-(1-g_{0})(\mu_{0}-\mu)\right\}\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi}\int\frac{g}{(1-g)}(1-\hat{g}_{n})(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu)\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi}\int(\mu-\psi)\left(\hat{g}_{n}-g_{0}\right)\,\mathrm{d}Q_{0}+\frac{1}{\pi}\int(\mu-\psi)\hat{g}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0}).

Therefore, adding and subtracting terms and using assumed bounds,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mu,g}\left|{\int\phi_{\mu,g}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\hat{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{L_{2}(Q_{0})}+\|\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{0}\|_{L_{2}(Q_{0})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{\mu,g}\left|{\int\frac{g}{(1-g)}(1-\hat{g}_{n})(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu)\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{\mu}\left|{\int(\mu-\psi)\hat{g}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\hat{Q}_{n}-Q_{0})}\right|.

Each of these terms is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by assumption. Thus,
\|\hat{P}_{n}-P_{0}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
.

Next, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}\phi_{\psi_{0},\hat{\eta}_{n}}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left\{\frac{\left(g_{0}-\hat{g}_{n}\right)\left(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{0}\right)}{\hat{\pi}_{n}\left(1-\hat{g}_{n}\right)}\right\}.

which is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(1)
by the assumed boundedness of
\hat{g}_{n}
away from zero and the consistency of
\hat{\mu}_{n}
and
\hat{g}_{n}
.

Next, we show that
\psi_{n}^{\circ}-\hat{\psi}_{n}=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. Use the definition of the estimating equations-based estimator construction
T_{2}
, we can see that

\displaystyle\psi_{n}^{\circ}

\displaystyle:=T_{2}(\eta_{n},\hat{P}_{n})=\hat{Q}_{n}\left\{\frac{g_{n}\left(1-\hat{g}_{n}\right)}{\hat{\pi}_{n}(1-g_{n})}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{n}\right)+\frac{\hat{g}_{n}}{\hat{\pi}_{n}}\mu_{n}\right\},

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{n}

\displaystyle:=T_{2}(\hat{\eta}_{n},\hat{P}_{n})=\hat{Q}_{n}\left\{\frac{\hat{g}_{n}}{\hat{\pi}_{n}}\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\},

\displaystyle\psi_{n}^{\circ}-\hat{\psi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}_{n}}\hat{Q}_{n}\left\{\frac{g_{n}-\hat{g}_{n}}{1-g_{n}}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{n}\right)\right\},

which is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. Finally,

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{n}\phi_{\hat{\psi}_{n},\eta_{n}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\hat{Q}_{n}\left\{\frac{\hat{g}_{n}-g_{n}^{*}}{\pi_{n}^{*}\left[1-g_{n}^{*}\right]}\left[\mu_{n}^{*}-\hat{\mu}_{n}\right]\right\}-\frac{\hat{\pi}_{n}}{\pi_{n}^{*}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\hat{\psi}_{n})

\displaystyle=\hat{Q}_{n}\left\{\frac{\hat{g}_{n}-g_{n}^{*}}{\pi_{n}^{*}\left[1-g_{n}^{*}\right]}\left[\mu_{n}^{*}-\hat{\mu}_{n}\right]\right\},

which is
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption as discussed above. Hence, condition
(B3)
holds for the estimating equations-based estimator.

\hfill\blacksquare

Supplement E
Lemmas supporting the proof of Proposition
5.3

We first present several simple algebraic identities that we will use repeatedly.

Lemma E.1
.

For any
\{Z_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}:i,j=1,2,\dotsc,n\}
such that
Z_{ij}=Z_{ji}
and
Z_{ii}=\tau_{f}
for all
i,j
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left[\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}\right]^{2}&=4\sum_{i\neq j,j\neq k,k\neq i}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}+2\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,i\neq k,i\neq l\\
j\neq k,j\neq l,k\neq l\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl},\text{ and}\end{split}

(23)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i,j,k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}&=2\tau_{f}\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}+\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}^{2}+n\tau_{f}^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j,i\neq k,j\neq k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}.\end{split}

(24)

Proof of
Lemma

E.1
.

We have

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,k\neq l\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,k\neq l\\
i=k,j\neq l\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,k\neq l\\
i=l,j\neq k\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,k\neq l\\
i\neq l,j=k\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,k\neq l\\
i\neq k,j=l\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,k\neq l\\
i=k,j=l\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,k\neq l\\
i=l,j=k\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,i\neq k,i\neq l\\
j\neq k,j\neq l,k\neq l\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{i\neq j,j\neq k,k\neq i}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}+2\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,i\neq k,i\neq l\\
j\neq k,j\neq l,k\neq l\end{subarray}}Z_{ij}Z_{kl},

which proves (
23
). We also have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=j\neq k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}+\sum_{i=k\neq j}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}+\sum_{i\neq j=k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}+\sum_{i=j=k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}+\sum_{i\neq j,i\neq k,j\neq k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik}

\displaystyle=2\tau_{f}\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}+\sum_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}^{2}+n\tau_{f}^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j,i\neq k,j\neq k}Z_{ij}Z_{ik},

which proves (
24
).

\hfill\blacksquare

We next present several non-asymptotic bounds for empirical and bootstrap empirical
V
-processes for a generic function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
satisfying
f(x,y)=f(y,x)
and
f(x,x)=\tau_{f}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Here,
f
may depend on
n
, but is assumed to be deterministic. We use the symbol
\lesssim
to mean “less than or equal up to a constant not depending on
n
.”

Lemma E.2
.

If
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
satisfies
f(x,y)=f(y,x)
and
f(x,x)=\tau_{f}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, then

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]-n^{-1}\tau_{f}\right\}^{2}\lesssim n^{-3}\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)+n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}.

Proof of
Lemma

E.2
.

For simplicity, we denote
V_{0}(x,y):=\int f\,\mathrm{d}\left[(\delta_{x}-P_{0})\times(\delta_{y}-P_{0})\right]
for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By the law of total expectation, for any
i\neq j
, we note that

\displaystyle E_{0}[V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})\mid X_{j}]

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left[f(X_{i},X_{j})-\int f(X_{i},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)+\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})\mid X_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)-\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})-\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)+\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})

\displaystyle=0

Hence,
E_{0}[V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})]=0
for
i\neq j
and
E_{0}[V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})V_{0}(X_{i},X_{k})]=0
for
i\neq j\neq k
.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{0}\left\{\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]-n^{-1}\tau_{f}\right\}^{2}\\
&\qquad=E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})-n^{-1}\tau_{f}\right\}^{2}\\
&\qquad\lesssim E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})-n^{-1}\tau_{f}\right\}^{2}+E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})\right\}^{2}.\end{split}

(25)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
25
), by the symmetry of
f
and the definition of
\tau_{f}
,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left\{-\frac{2}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})+\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left\{-\frac{2}{n}\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(y)-\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(y)\right\}^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}}\mathrm{Var}\left(\left[\int f(x,X_{1})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]\right)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{3}}\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}.

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
25
), by
Lemma

E.1
and properties of
V_{0}
,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{0}\left[\frac{4}{n^{4}}\sum_{i\neq j,j\neq k,k\neq i}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})V_{0}(X_{i},X_{k})+\frac{2}{n^{4}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,i\neq k,i\neq l\\
j\neq k,j\neq l,k\neq l\end{subarray}}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})V_{0}(X_{k},X_{l})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{0}\left[\frac{2}{n^{4}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[V_{0}(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})-\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)+\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})-\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}\left[\int f\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})-\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}.

For the right hand side of previous display, we note that

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})-\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{0}[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}]+E_{0}\left[\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]^{2}+E_{0}\left[\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2E_{0}\left[\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]^{2}-2E_{0}\left[\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2E_{0}\left[\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]\left[\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{0}[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}]-E_{0}\left[\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]^{2}-E_{0}\left[\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}+2[(P_{0}\times P_{0})f]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq E_{0}[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}],

where the last inequality is because
[(P_{0}\times P_{0})f]^{2}\leq E_{0}\left[\int f(X_{1},y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]^{2}
and
[(P_{0}\times P_{0})f]^{2}\leq E_{0}\left[\int f(x,X_{2})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}
by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. This implies
E_{0}\left[n^{-2}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{0}(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}\lesssim n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Lemma E.3
.

If
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
satisfies
f(x,y)=f(y,x)
and
f(x,x)=\tau_{f}
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, then for the empirical bootstrap
\hat{P}_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{n}
,
E_{0}E_{W}\left[\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right]^{2}\lesssim n^{-3}\tau_{f}^{2}+n^{-3}\tau_{f}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.3
.

For simplicity, we denote
g_{n}(y):=\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x)
. We then have

\displaystyle\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})g_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[g_{n}(X_{i}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}]

Since
X_{1}^{*},\dotsc,X_{n}^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\mathrm{iid}}}}{{\sim}}\mathbb{P}_{n}
,
E_{W}[g_{n}(X_{i}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}][g_{n}(X_{j}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}]=0
for any
i\neq j
, so

\displaystyle E_{W}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[g_{n}(X_{i}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}]\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=E_{W}\left[\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{n}[g_{n}(X_{i}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}][g_{n}(X_{j}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}]\right]

\displaystyle=E_{W}\left[\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}^{n}[g_{n}(X_{i}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}][g_{n}(X_{j}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}]\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}E_{W}[g_{n}(X_{i}^{*})-\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}^{2}-(\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}^{2}.

We then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}^{2}&=\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i,j,k}f(X_{i},X_{j})f(X_{i},X_{k})-\frac{2}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\left[\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}\left[\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}.\end{split}

(26)

By
Lemma

E.1
, the expectation of the first term on the right-hand side of (
26
) is

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i,j,k}f(X_{i},X_{j})f(X_{i},X_{k})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=E_{0}\left[\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})+\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}+\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i\neq j,i\neq k,j\neq k}f(X_{i},X_{j})f(X_{i},X_{k})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2(n-1)\tau_{f}}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})\right]+\frac{n-1}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right]+\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{n^{2}}E_{0}[f(X_{1},X_{2})f(X_{1},X_{3})].

The expectation of the second term on the right-hand side of (
26
) is

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\frac{2}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\left[\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{0}\left\{\frac{2}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\left[\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]\right\}+E_{0}\left\{\frac{2}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\left[\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})\right]+\frac{2(n-1)}{n}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})f(X_{1},X_{3})\right].

The expectation of the third term on the right-hand side of (
26
) is

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}\left[\int f(x,X_{j})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\right\}=E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})f(X_{1},X_{3})\right].

Combining these calculations, we get

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{1}{n}E_{0}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}g_{n}^{2}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|{-\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})\right]+\frac{n-1}{n^{3}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right]+\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{3}}-\frac{n-2}{n^{3}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})f(X_{1},X_{3})\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}E_{0}|f(X_{1},X_{2})|+\frac{1}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right]+\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{3}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}E_{0}|f(X_{1},X_{2})f(X_{1},X_{3})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}E_{0}|f(X_{1},X_{2})|+\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right]+\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{3}},

where the last inequality is due to

\displaystyle E_{0}|f(X_{1},X_{2})f(X_{1},X_{3})|

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left\{E_{0}[|f(X_{1},X_{2})|\mid X_{1}]E_{0}[|f(X_{1},X_{3})|\mid X_{1}]\right\}

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left\{\left(E_{0}[|f(X_{1},X_{2})|\mid X_{1}]\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq E_{0}\left\{E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\mid X_{1}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left[f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}\right].

The result follows.

\hfill\blacksquare

Lemma E.4
.

If
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
satisfies
f(x,y)=f(y,x)
and
f(x,x)=\tau_{f}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, then for the empirical bootstrap
\hat{P}_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{n}
,

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-n^{-1}\tau_{f}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim n^{-3}\tau_{f}^{2}+n^{-3}\tau_{f}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}+n^{-3}\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}P_{0}(y).

Proof of
Lemma

E.4
.

We define

\displaystyle V_{n}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\int f\,\mathrm{d}\left[(\delta_{x}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\delta_{y}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\right]

\displaystyle=f(x,y)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left[f(x,X_{k})+f(y,X_{k})\right]+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{n}f(X_{k},X_{l}).

By the law of total expectation, for any
i\neq j
,

\displaystyle E_{W}\left[V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})\mid X_{j}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle=E_{W}\left[f(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\{f(X_{i}^{*},X_{k})+f(X_{j}^{*},X_{k})\right\}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{n}f(X_{k},X_{l})\mid X_{j}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{i},X_{j}^{*})-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,k=1}^{n}f(X_{i},X_{k})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f(X_{j}^{*},X_{k})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{n}f(X_{k},X_{l})

\displaystyle=0.

Hence,
E_{W}[V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})]=0
for
i\neq j
and
E_{W}[V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{k}^{*})]=0
for
i\neq j\neq k
.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}\right\}^{2}\\
&\qquad=E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}\right\}^{2}\\
&\qquad\lesssim E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}\right\}^{2}+E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})\right\}^{2}\\
\end{split}

(27)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
27
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\delta_{X_{i}^{*}}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\delta_{X_{i}^{*}}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{2}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*})+\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{2}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}[\mathbb{P}_{n}\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{2}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\frac{2}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}[P_{0}\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}).

Hence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}\right\}^{2}\\
&\qquad\lesssim E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right\}^{2}+E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}[P_{0}\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right\}^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad+E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})\right\}^{2}.\end{split}

(28)

By
Lemma

E.3
, the first term on the right-hand side of (
28
) is bounded up to a constant by
n^{-5}\tau_{f}^{2}+n^{-5}\tau_{f}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+n^{-4}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}
. For the second term on the right-hand side of (
28
),

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}[P_{0}\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}}E_{0}\left[\mathrm{Var}_{W}\left(\int f(x,X_{1}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{3}}E_{0}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int f(x,X_{i})\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{3}}\int\left(\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y).

For the last term on the right-hand side of (
28
), we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}f(X_{i},X_{j})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left\{\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\{\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}E_{0}\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})+\frac{1}{n^{4}}E_{0}\left[\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{4}}+\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}E_{0}f(X_{1},X_{2})+\frac{1}{n^{6}}E_{0}\left[\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{4}}+\frac{2\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+\frac{1}{n^{6}}E_{0}\left[\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}.

By
Lemma

E.1
, we note that

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left[4\sum_{i\neq j,j\neq k,k\neq i}f(X_{i},X_{j})f(X_{i},X_{k})+2\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,i\neq k,i\neq l\\
j\neq k,j\neq l,k\neq l\end{subarray}}f(X_{i},X_{j})f(X_{k},X_{l})\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{3}E_{0}f(X_{1},X_{2})f(X_{1},X_{3})+n^{2}E_{0}f(X_{1},X_{2})^{2}+n^{4}[E_{0}f(X_{1},X_{2})]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{3}\int\left(\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)+n^{2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}+n^{4}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}.

Combining these calculations, we get

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{4}}+\frac{\tau_{f}}{n^{3}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{4}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{n^{3}}\int\left(\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y).

Therefore,

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})-\frac{\tau_{f}}{n}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{f}^{2}n^{-4}+n^{-3}\tau_{f}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{-4}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}+n^{-3}\int\left(\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y).

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
27
), by
Lemma

E.1
and the properties of
V_{n}
derived above,

\displaystyle E_{W}\left[\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{W}\left[\frac{4}{n^{4}}\sum_{i\neq j,j\neq k,k\neq i}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{k}^{*})+\frac{2}{n^{4}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j,i\neq k,i\neq l\\
j\neq k,j\neq l,k\neq l\end{subarray}}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})V_{n}(X_{k}^{*},X_{l}^{*})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{W}\left[\frac{2}{n^{4}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{W}\left[V_{n}(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{W}\left[f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})-\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)+(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})f\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{W}\left[f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})-\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)\right]^{2}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}\left[(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})f\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{W}\left[f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})-\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)\right]^{2}.

Note that

\displaystyle E_{W}\left[f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})-\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)-\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{W}\left\{f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})^{2}+\left[\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)\right]^{2}+\left[\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)\right]^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-2f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})\left[\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)\right]-2f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})\left[\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+2\left[\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)\right]\left[\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=E_{W}\left\{f(X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*})^{2}-\left[\int f(X_{1}^{*},y)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(y)\right]^{2}-\left[\int f(x,X_{2}^{*})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(x)\right]^{2}+2\left[\int f\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})\right]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}),

where the last inequality is because
\left[n^{-2}\sum_{i,j}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}\leq n^{-1}\sum_{j}\left[n^{-1}\sum_{i}f(X_{i},X_{j})\right]^{2}
by Jensen’s inequality. Hence,

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left[\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}V_{n}(X_{i}^{*},X_{j}^{*})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{0}\int f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}}E_{0}\left[\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=j}f(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}f(X_{i},X_{j})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\tau_{f}^{2}}{n^{3}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int f^{2}\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0}),

and the result follows.

\hfill\blacksquare

In the next few results, we assume that
K:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is a kernel function satisfying
K(-x)=K(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\int K(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1
, and
\int K(x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
. Notably,
K
need not be non-negative or have compact support. We also let
h=h_{n}>0
be a sequence of bandwidths. For simplicity, we assume the bandwidth matrix is
hI_{d}
, though the results can be extended to more general bandwidth matrices. For some results, we require that
K
is an
m
th order kernel function, by which we mean that
\int z^{\alpha}K(z)\,\mathrm{d}z=0
for all
\alpha
such that
1\leq|\alpha|\leq m-1
and
\int|z^{\alpha}K(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z<\infty
for all
\alpha
such that
|\alpha|=m
.

As in the main text, we define
K_{h}(x,y):=h^{-d}K\left(h^{-1}(x-y)\right)
for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We define the functions
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}
, each depending on
K
and
h
, as
f_{1}:(x,y)\mapsto K_{h}(x,y)
and
f_{2}:(x,y)\mapsto\int K_{h}(x,z)K_{h}(y,z)\,\mathrm{d}z
.

Corollary E.5
.

If
P_{0}
possesses uniformly bounded Lebesgue density function
\eta_{0}
, then
\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
for each
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
. If in addition
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
, then
\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]=n^{-1}\tau_{f}+o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]=n^{-1}\tau_{f}+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

Proof of
Corollary

E.5
.

We first show that
\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}=O(1)
,
\int\left\{\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)=O(1)
, and
\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}=O(h^{-d/2})
for each
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
. Note that

\displaystyle\left\|{f_{1}}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}

\displaystyle=\iint\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left|{K\left(\frac{x-y}{h}\right)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\iint\left|{K(s)}\right|\eta_{0}(sh+y)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\left|{K(s)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\|K\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}<\infty,\text{ and}

\displaystyle\left\|{f_{2}}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}

\displaystyle=\iint\frac{1}{h^{2d}}\left|{\int K\left(\frac{x-z}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{y-z}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}z}\right|\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\iint\left|{K\left(s\right)K\left(t\right)}\right|\left[\int\eta_{0}(z+hs)\eta_{0}(z+ht)\,\mathrm{d}z\right]\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\int\left|{K\left(s\right)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{2}\leq\|K\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}<\infty.

Next,

\displaystyle\int\left[\int f_{1}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\int\left[\frac{1}{h^{d}}\int K\left(\frac{x-y}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\int\left[\int K\left(s\right)\eta_{0}(sh+y)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|K\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}<\infty,\text{ and}

\displaystyle\int\left[\int f_{2}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\int\left[\frac{1}{h^{2d}}\iint K\left(\frac{x-z}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{y-z}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\int\left[\iint K\left(s\right)K\left(t\right)\eta_{0}(y-ht+hs)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|K\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2}<\infty.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\left\|{f_{1}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h^{2d}}\iint K^{2}\left(\frac{x-y}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h^{d}}\iint K^{2}\left(s\right)\eta_{0}(sh+y)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\|K\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\left\|{f_{2}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\iint\left[\frac{1}{h^{2d}}\int K\left(\frac{x-z}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{y-z}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}z\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h^{4d}}\iiiint K\left(\frac{x-z}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{y-z}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{x-w}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{y-w}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}w\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h^{2d}}\iiiint K\left(s\right)K\left(s+\frac{y-x}{h}\right)K\left(t\right)K\left(t+\frac{y-x}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h^{d}}\iiiint K\left(s\right)K\left(r\right)K\left(t\right)K\left(t+r-s\right)\eta_{0}(y-h(r-s))\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{h^{d}}\iiint\left|{K\left(s\right)K\left(r\right)K\left(t\right)K\left(t+r-s\right)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{h^{d}}\iint\left|{K\left(s\right)K\left(r\right)}\right|\left[\int K^{2}\left(t\right)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}\left[\int K^{2}\left(t+r-s\right)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left[\int\left|{K\left(s\right)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{2}\left[\int K^{2}\left(t\right)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{h^{d}}\left\|{K}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda)}^{4},

which implies that
\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}=O(h^{-d/2})
for each
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
as claimed.

We note that
f(x,y)=f(y,x)
for each
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
because
K(-u)=K(u)
by assumption, and
\tau_{f_{1}}=h^{-d}K(0)
and
\tau_{f_{2}}=h^{-d}\int K^{2}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which are both
O(h^{-d})
. Hence, using the results above, by
Lemma

E.3
, for each
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
,

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-2d}n^{-3}+h^{-d}n^{-3}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-2d}n^{-3}+h^{-d}n^{-3}+n^{-2}h^{-d}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-1}(nh^{d})^{-2}+n^{-1}(nh^{d})^{-1},

which is
o(n^{-1})
if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
. This implies that
\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
. Since
\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)=O(1)
, by
Lemma

E.2
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]-n^{-1}\tau_{f}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-3}\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)+n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-3}+n^{-1}(nh^{d})^{-1},

which is
o(n^{-1})
if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
. This implies that
\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]=n^{-1}\tau_{f}+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
. Since
\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)=O(1)
, by
Lemma

E.4
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left\{\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-n^{-1}\tau_{f}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim n^{-3}\tau_{f}^{2}+n^{-3}\tau_{f}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}+n^{-2}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}^{2}+n^{-3}\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim n^{-3}h^{-2d}+n^{-3}h^{-d}+n^{-2}h^{-d}+n^{-3},

which is
o(n^{-1})
if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
. This implies that
\int f\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]=n^{-1}\tau_{f}+o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Lemma E.6
.

Suppose that the density function
\eta_{0}
of
P_{0}
is
m
-times continuously differentiable with
\int[(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
for all
\alpha
such that
|\alpha|=m
, and
K
is an
m
th order kernel. Then for each
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}

\displaystyle\int\left[\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=O(h^{2m}).

If in addition
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|D^{\alpha}\eta_{0}(x)\right|<\infty
, then
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|{\int f(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)}\right|=O(h^{m})
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.6
.

Since
\eta_{0}
is
m
-times continuously differentiable, for all
u
, a Taylor expansion with the Laplacian representation of the remainder gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\eta_{0}(x+u)-\eta_{0}(x)\\
&\qquad=\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq m-1}\frac{1}{\alpha!}u^{\alpha}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)+\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m}{\alpha!}u^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{1}(1-r)^{m-1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+ru)\,\mathrm{d}r.\end{split}

(29)

Hence, for
f=f_{1}
and any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\int f_{1}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{1}{h^{d}}K\left(\frac{x-y}{h}\right)\eta_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y-\eta_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\int K(s)[\eta_{0}(x+hs)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq m-1}\frac{1}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\int s^{\alpha}K(s)\,\mathrm{d}s(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\iint_{0}^{1}s^{\alpha}K(s)(1-r)^{m-1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+rhs)\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}s.

Since
K
is an
m
th order kernel function, the first term on the right hand size is zero. Defining
H(x,s,\alpha):=\int_{0}^{1}(1-r)^{m-1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+rhs)\,\mathrm{d}r
, we then have

\displaystyle\int\left[\int f_{1}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=\int\left[\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\int s^{\alpha}K(s)H(x,s,\alpha)\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{|\alpha|,|\beta|=m}\frac{m^{2}}{\alpha!\beta!}h^{|\alpha|}h^{|\beta|}\iiint s^{\alpha}t^{\beta}K(s)K(t)H(x,s,\alpha)H(x,t,\beta)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{|\alpha|,|\beta|=m}\frac{m^{2}}{\alpha!\beta!}h^{2m}\iint\left|{s^{\alpha}t^{\beta}K(s)K(t)}\right|\left[\int H(x,s,\alpha)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right]^{1/2}\left[\int H(x,t,\beta)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t.

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int H(x,s,\alpha)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\left[\int_{0}^{1}(1-r)^{m-1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+rhs)\,\mathrm{d}r\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int\left[\int_{0}^{1}(1-r)^{2(m-1)}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]\left[\int_{0}^{1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+rhs)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{0}^{1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+rhs)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int[(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

which is finite by assumption. Hence,

\displaystyle\int\left[\int f_{1}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2m}\sum_{|\alpha|,|\beta|=m}\iint\left|{s^{\alpha}t^{\beta}K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=h^{2m}\left[\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\int\left|{s^{\alpha}K(s)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{2}=O(h^{2m}).

If
D^{\alpha}\eta_{0}
is uniformly bounded, then

\displaystyle\left|{\int f_{1}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|{\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\iint_{0}^{1}s^{\alpha}K(s)(1-r)^{m-1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+rhs)\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}s}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left\|D^{\alpha}\eta_{0}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{mh^{m}}{\alpha!}\int|s^{\alpha}K(s)|\left[\int_{0}^{1}(1-r)^{m-1}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad=\left\|D^{\alpha}\eta_{0}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{h^{m}}{\alpha!}\int|s^{\alpha}K(s)|\,\mathrm{d}s,

which is independent of
x
and order of
h^{m}
.

For
f=f_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\int f_{2}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad=\iint\frac{1}{h^{2d}}K\left(\frac{x-z}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{y-z}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad=\iint\frac{1}{h^{d}}K\left(s\right)K\left(s+\frac{y-x}{h}\right)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad=\iint K(s)K(t)\eta_{0}(x+h(t-s))\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t-\eta_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad=\iint K(s)K(t)[\eta_{0}(x+h(t-s))-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq m-1}\frac{1}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\iint(t-s)^{\alpha}K(s)K(t)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\iiint_{0}^{1}(t-s)^{\alpha}K(s)K(t)(1-r)^{m-1}(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x+rh(t-s))\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t,

where the first term on the right hand size is zero because
K
is an
m
th order kernel function. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\int\left[\int f_{2}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=\int\left[\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\iint(t-s)^{\alpha}K(s)K(t)H(x,s-t,\alpha)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{|\alpha|,|\beta|=m}\frac{m^{2}}{\alpha!\beta!}h^{|\alpha|}h^{|\beta|}\iiiint(t-s)^{\alpha}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})^{\beta}K(s)K(s^{\prime})K(t)K(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left[\int H(x,s-t,\alpha)H(x,s^{\prime}-t^{\prime},\beta)\,\mathrm{d}x\right]\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|,|\beta|=m}h^{2m}\iiiint\left|{(t-s)^{\alpha}(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})^{\beta}K(s)K(s^{\prime})K(t)K(t^{\prime})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left[\int H(x,s-t,\alpha)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right]^{1/2}\left[\int H(x,s^{\prime}-t^{\prime},\beta)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim h^{2m}\left[\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\iint\left|{(t-s)^{\alpha}K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim h^{2m}\left[\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\iint\left|{\prod_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{k_{i}=0}^{\alpha_{i}}t_{i}^{k_{i}}s_{i}^{\alpha_{i}-k_{i}}\right)K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq h^{2m}\left[\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\iint\left|{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\beta\leq\alpha\\
0\leq\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}t^{\beta}s^{\gamma}K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq h^{2m}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq|\beta|\leq m\\
0\leq|\gamma|\leq m\end{subarray}}\iint\left|{t^{\beta}s^{\gamma}K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=h^{2m}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq|\beta|\leq m\end{subarray}}\int\left|{t^{\beta}K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{4},

which is
O(h^{2m})
because
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|=m\end{subarray}}\int\left|{t^{\beta}K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}t<\infty
by assumption, and for any
0\leq\beta<m
there exists
\alpha
with
|\alpha|=m
such that

\displaystyle\int\left|{t^{\beta}K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{[-1,1]^{d}}\left|{t^{\beta}K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash[-1,1]^{d}}\left|{t^{\beta}K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int\left|{K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}t+\int\left|{t^{\alpha}K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}t,

which is finite by assumption.

If
D^{\alpha}\eta_{0}
is uniformly bounded, then

\displaystyle\left|{\int f_{2}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left\|D^{\alpha}\eta_{0}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{m}{\alpha!}h^{|\alpha|}\iint\left|{(t-s)^{\alpha}K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\int_{0}^{1}(1-r)^{m-1}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad=\left\|D^{\alpha}\eta_{0}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{|\alpha|=m}\frac{h^{m}}{\alpha!}\iint\left|{(t-s)^{\alpha}K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t.

We showed the integral in the final expression is finite above, so the expression is independent of
x
and order of
h^{m}
.

\hfill\blacksquare

We now consider kernel density estimators
\eta_{n}
and
\eta_{n}^{*}
with common bandwidth
h
and kernel
K
, i.e.
\eta_{n}(x)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{h}(x,X_{i})
and
\eta_{n}^{*}(x)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{h}(x,X_{i}^{*})
.

Lemma E.7
.

Suppose that the density function
\eta_{0}
of
P_{0}
is uniformly bounded and
m
-times continuously differentiable with
\int[(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
for all
\alpha
such that
|\alpha|=m
and
K
is an
m
-th order kernel function. Then
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
. If in addition
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
holds, then
(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.7
.

To show the first statement, we note that

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})

\displaystyle=\int K_{h}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})],

which is
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
by
Corollary

E.5
with
f=f_{1}
.

To show the second statement, by adding and subtracting terms and defining
\eta_{0,h}(x)=\int K_{h}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n})+(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0,h})+(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0})-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0,h})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad=\int K_{h}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]+\int K_{h}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int[K_{h}(x,y)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)]-\int K_{h}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\int[K_{h}(x,y)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)].

By
Corollary

E.5
with
f=f_{1}
,

\int K_{h}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2}),

and if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow 0
, then

\int K_{h}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\int K_{h}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2}).

Finally, we write

\displaystyle\int[K_{h}(x,y)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)]

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})g_{h},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\int[K_{h}(x,y)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)]

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})g_{h}

for
g_{h}(x):=\int[K_{h}(x,y)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)
. As in the proofs of
Lemma

E.3
and
Lemma

E.4
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})g_{h}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2},\text{ and}

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})g_{h}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}E_{0}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}=n^{-1}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2}.

Since
\int g_{h}(x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=O(h^{2m})
by
Lemma

E.6
, we have
\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2}\longrightarrow 0
as long as
h\longrightarrow 0
and
P_{0}
possesses uniformly bounded density. This implies that
\int[K_{h}(x,y)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)]=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
and
\int[K_{h}(x,y)-\eta_{0}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Lemma E.8
.

Suppose that the density function
\eta_{0}
of
P_{0}
is uniformly bounded and
m
-times continuously differentiable with
\int[(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
for all
\alpha
such that
|\alpha|=m
and
K
is an
m
-th order kernel function. If
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
and
nh^{4m}\longrightarrow 0
, then
2\int[\eta_{n}(x)-\eta_{0}(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int[\eta_{n}^{*}(x)-\eta_{0}(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
holds for the empirical bootstrap.

Proof of
Lemma

E.8
.

Recall that
f_{1}(x,y):=K_{h}\left(x,y\right)
and
f_{2}(x,y):=\int K_{h}(x,z)K_{h}(y,z)\,\mathrm{d}z
. We denote
\eta_{0,h}(x):=\int K_{h}\left(x,y\right)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)
for simplicity. We then note that

\displaystyle\int\eta_{n}^{2}=\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}),\ \int\eta_{n}^{*2}=\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}),\ \int\eta_{0,h}^{2}=\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})

\displaystyle\int\eta_{n}^{*}\eta_{n}=\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}),\ \int\eta_{n}^{*}\eta_{0,h}=\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\times P_{0}),\ \int\eta_{n}\eta_{0,h}=\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times P_{0}).

Hence, by adding and subtracting term, we have

\displaystyle\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{0})^{2}-2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}+\eta_{n}-\eta_{0,h}+\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0})^{2}-2\int(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0,h}+\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int\left(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}\right)^{2}-\int\left(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0,h}\right)^{2}-\int\left(\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0}\right)^{2}+2\int\left(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}\right)\left(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0,h}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\int\left(\eta_{n}^{*}-\eta_{n}\right)\left(\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0}\right)-2\int\left(\eta_{n}-\eta_{0,h}\right)\left(\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]-\int\left(\eta_{0,h}-\eta_{0}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]+2\int[f_{2}(x,y)-\eta_{0,h}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\int[f_{2}(x,y)-\eta_{0,h}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y).

By
Corollary

E.5
,
2\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})
, and if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow 0
, then

\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2}).

By
Lemma

E.6
, the third term on the right is
O(h^{2m})
, which is
o(n^{-1/2})
if
nh^{4m}\longrightarrow 0
. Finally, we write

\displaystyle\int[f_{2}(x,y)-\eta_{0,h}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})g_{h},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\int[f_{2}(x,y)-\eta_{0,h}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})g_{h}

for
g_{h}(x):=\int[f_{2}(x,y)-\eta_{0,h}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)=\int[f_{2}(x,y)-f_{1}(x,y)]\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)
. As in the proofs of
Lemma

E.3
and
Lemma

E.4
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})g_{h}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2},\text{ and}

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})g_{h}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}E_{0}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}=n^{-1}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2}.

By
Lemma

E.6
, we have

\displaystyle\int\left\{\int[f_{2}(x,y)-f_{1}(x,y)]\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\int\left\{\int f_{2}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int\left\{\int f_{1}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=O(h^{2m}).

This implies
\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2}=O(h^{2m})=o(n^{-1/2})
since
nh^{4m}\longrightarrow 0
and
P_{0}
possesses uniformly bounded density. Hence,
\int[f_{2}(x,y)-\eta_{0,h}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)=o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-3/4})
and
\int[f_{2}(x,y)-\eta_{0,h}(x)]\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-3/4})
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Lemma E.9
.

Suppose that the density function
\eta_{0}
of
P_{0}
is uniformly bounded and
m
-times continuously differentiable with
\int[(D^{\alpha}\eta_{0})(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
for all
\alpha
such that
|\alpha|=m
and
K
is an
m
-th order kernel function. If
nh^{d}\longrightarrow\infty
and
nh^{2m}\longrightarrow 0
, then
2\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}-\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}=o_{P_{W}}(n^{-1/2})
holds for the empirical bootstrap.

Proof of
Lemma

E.9
.

Recall that
f_{1}(x,y):=K_{h}\left(x,y\right)
and
f_{2}(x,y):=\int K_{h}(x,z)K_{h}(y,z)\,\mathrm{d}z
. We define
f_{3}:=f_{1}-f_{2}
. We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}=2\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}),\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}=2\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\times\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}).

Hence, by adding and subtracting terms,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}\phi_{n}^{*}-2\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n}\right)/2

\displaystyle\qquad=\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}+\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0}+P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n}+\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0}+P_{0})]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0}+P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0}+P_{0})]

\displaystyle\qquad=\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]-\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]+2\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times P_{0}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times P_{0}].

By
Corollary

E.5
,

\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]=\int f_{1}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]-\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2})

and if
nh^{d}\longrightarrow 0
, then

\displaystyle\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]

\displaystyle\qquad=\left\{\int f_{1}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\int f_{1}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\left\{\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})]-\int f_{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\times(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})]\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1/2}).

Finally, we define
g_{h}(x):=\int f_{3}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)
. As in the proofs of
Lemma

E.3
and
Lemma

E.4
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})g_{h}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\left\|{g_{h}}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2},\text{ and}

\displaystyle E_{0}E_{W}\left[(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})g_{h}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}E_{0}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}=n^{-1}\|g_{h}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}^{2}.

By
Lemma

E.6
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int g_{h}(x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x&=\int\left\{\int[f_{1}(x,y)-f_{2}(x,y)]\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq 2\int\left\{\int f_{1}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int\left\{\int f_{2}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)-\eta_{0}(x)\right\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

(30)

which is
O(h^{2m})
. This implies that
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})g_{h}
and
(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{*}-\mathbb{P}_{n})g_{h}
are
o_{P_{0}^{*}}(n^{-1})
and
o_{P_{W}^{*}}(n^{-1})
, respectively, since
nh^{2m}\longrightarrow 0
and
P_{0}
possesses uniformly bounded density. Finally, (
30
) also implies that
\int f_{3}\,\mathrm{d}(P_{0}\times P_{0})=O(h^{m})=o(n^{-1/2})
since
nh^{2m}\longrightarrow 0
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Supplement F
Lemmas supporting the proof of Proposition
5.5

For any
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, we define

G[f](x,y):=\iint f(s,t)K_{h}(s,x)K_{h}(t,y)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t=\iint f(x+hu,y+hv)K(u)K(v)\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}v.

If
f
is symmetric, which is the case for
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
, then
G[f]
is symmetric as well. If
f(x+c,y+c)=f(x,y)
holds for any
c\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which is also the case for
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
, then

\displaystyle G[f](x,x)=\iint f(x+hu,x+hv)K(u)K(v)\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}v=\iint f(hu,hv)K(u)K(v)\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}v,

which does not depend on
x
. For simplicity, we denote
\tau_{G[f]}=G[f](x,x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Lemma F.1
.

Suppose that
P_{0}
possesses uniformly bounded density. If
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
satisfies
f(x+c,y+c)=f(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}
for all
x,y,c\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\int[K(x)]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty
, then
\left\|{G[f]}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}\lesssim\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(\lambda\times P_{0})}
,
\left\|{G[f]}\right\|_{L_{2}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}\lesssim\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda\times P_{0})}
, and
\int\left[\int G[f](x,y)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\lesssim\int\left[\int|f(x,y)|\,\mathrm{d}x\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)
, where the constants depend on
K
and
P_{0}
. For
f\in\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
,
\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{1}(\lambda\times P_{0})}=O(1)
,
\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{2}(\lambda\times\nu)}^{2}=O(\nu(\mathbb{R}^{d})h^{-d})
, and
\int\left[\int|f(x,y)|\,\mathrm{d}x\right]^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu(y)\lesssim\nu(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for a constant only depending on
K
and any finite measure
\nu
.

Proof of
Lemma

F.1
.

By the property of
f
, the boundedness of the density of
P_{0}
, and the assumption that
\int K^{2}<\infty
,

\displaystyle\left\|{G[f]}\right\|_{L_{1}(P_{0}\times P_{0})}

\displaystyle=\iint\left|{G[f](x,y)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=\iint\left|{\iint f(x+sh,y+th)K(s)K(t)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t}\right|\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\iint\left[\iint\left|{f(x+sh,y+th)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]\left|{K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\iint\left[\iint\left|{f(x+sh-th,y)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]\left|{K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint\left[\iint\left|{f(x^{\prime},y)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}P_{0}(y)\right]\left|{K(s)K(t)}\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t
